18	- **Rewrite roadmap step 4 IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-21 session, committed after steps 1-3 at 6f898d3).** \`src/stage2/compete.rs\` (stage 2.3(b) scoped fit competition): regions the whole-region ladder fails go to seed-first multi-type competition. Seeds = region faces jet-interior-first (ascending \`neighbor_jet_mismatch\`); per seed grow each jet-pruned candidate (\`grow::<S>\`) and commit the LARGEST validated area (DOF tiebreak); claim resolution assigns multi-claimed junction faces to the best claimant (2× margin); leftovers → per-facet. \`grow\` = seed_patch (jet-similar, DEPTH-LIMITED BFS \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH=3\`, cap 32, magnitude within 1.5×) → fit → \`flood\` (connected region faces validating the fit) → refit fixpoint. THREE hard-won fixes: (1) **flood tolerance = 30× vertex-tolerance** (\`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR\`) — a fit from partial support has ~1e-4mm residual and a coarse "flat" reads ~1e-3mm plane residual, both >> the 5e-5mm vtol yet << the ~0.1mm a neighbor diverges one row past a tangent junction; committed hyps are refit on full support (~1e-6mm) and claim resolution fixes one-row leaks. Without this, EVERYTHING returned NO GROWTH (seed failed its own fit at 5e-5). (2) **depth-limited seed patch** — a 48-face jet patch STRADDLED the cone↔cylinder tangent junction (both developable, k1 continuous across the seam) → fit 0.9mm off → seed fails; a shallow local patch stays on one surface (rounded_cube fillets never had this — they border spheres/planes = different curvature class, excluded). (3) **large-leftover-blob fallback** (\`LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX=8\` in classify.rs) — if competition leaves a connected leftover blob >8 faces (a whole sub-surface it couldn't fit: coarse fillet, or concave torus fillet awaiting step 5), DISCARD competition and per-facet the whole region → clean polyhedron, because an analytic surface islanded among per-facet planes reconstructs to INVALID wires at their jagged boundary (rounded_cube_coarse: 43 BRepCheck failures → 0). RESULTS: rounded_cube_10_r2_fine → 6 planar + 12 cyl + 8 sphere (=26, ref's 26), nosecone_medium → cone+sphere+base (=3, ref's 3); both build 1 valid solid at 0mm distance to reference (vol rel 4e-4 / 2e-3). Sharp-edged models unaffected (pass ladder, 0 competition regions). Net: stage4_integration 25→27 (nosecone_medium + rounded_cube pass), stage2/stage3 unchanged (130/99), NO regressions. New tests/stage2_competition.rs (2 tests: rounded_cube 6+12+8, nosecone cone+sphere+base + total-coverage). BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE env var logs per-commit + NO-GROWTH (kept for step 5). 24	- **STAGE 2 ARCHITECTURE PIVOT (2026-07-17, plan-only so far — code still implements the old BFS pipeline).** User concluded development had become heuristics-on-heuristics and asked for a back-to-basics restructure; DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md was fully rewritten (committed 703bead) around **partition-then-classify**, jumping directly — ONE-SHOT transition per user: no reconciliation of old/new stage numbers; when 2.1/2.2 land, new numbering owns --stage/--viz-stages and the old BFS pipeline is nerfed in place (disconnected from stage2(), deleted later); suite red until 2.3-2.5 land. Viz plan verified against viz.rs: boundary edges = class-colored LineOverlay segments (EdgeHighlight outlines whole faces — wrong primitive, stays TODO's delete candidate); regions = per-region FaceHighlight, deterministic palette. Pipeline: 2.1 fit-free edge classification (sharp dihedral OR signed two-sided curvature-jump descriptor σ_max/σ_min per face, J=0.5 local-relative + κ_floor ~ C·vtol/d²; two-sided catches the capsule sphere↔cylinder equal-σ_max case via σ_min) → 2.2 connected-region extraction (union-find; single facet = valid region) → 2.3 per-region classification ladder ascending DOF (plane 3 → sphere 4 → cylinder 5 → cone 6 → torus 7 → freeform; first fit validating all vertices at vtol + centroids at stol + orientation coherence wins; fits see whole region) → 2.4 repair loop (merge = tight-absorb-rule-at-vtol reborn; split = re-run 2.1 at J/2, J/4… inside failing regions, prefer all-analytic splits over whole-region freeform to avoid erasing missed seams; boundary relabel = promoted 2.9 with 2× margin + connectivity re-split) → 2.5 SelectedSurface output (stage-3 contract unchanged). Terminal fallback = per-facet planes (planar-only mode = \`--surface-types=planar\`, replacing --skip-stage). Rationale anchors: exactness makes validation decisive; tessellators seam B-rep boundaries even at tangencies (nosecone probe) so the true partition's boundaries are mesh edges; accidental sub-region fits (sphere bands on cones/tori) become structurally impossible, deleting stage ordering, seeding masks, coverage acceptance tests, greedy selection, absorb. Plan has a Rewrite Roadmap (edges.rs/regions.rs/classify.rs/repair.rs; bfs.rs deleted; EdgeHighlight viz finally wired for 2.1) an Abandoned Approaches section explaining each retired mechanism, and a Contingency section (seed-first multi-type competition: invert the old loops — per-seed all-types fit-and-expand, largest-area-with-DOF-tiebreak wins, expansion through claimed faces allowed; sound via the sub-region lemma; not chosen because small-seed conditioning and freeform stay unsolved; roadmap step 1's calibration gate is the trigger to switch). The contingency's required upgrade (user's idea, 2026-07-17): degenerate-limit growth model — parameterize torus by spine curvature k=1/R so k=0 IS the cylinder (Pratt/Taubin-style), grow at k≈0 with ridge-toward-0 until data identifies the bend; needs a chordal refit trigger (the 10× REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER window is the real strip-problem blocker, neighbor strips sit ~R·Δφ²/2 ≫ 10×vtol off the fit); torus-only generalization would tile spheres with bands, so the endpoint is the full SOR/Dupin-cyclide family with classification as model reduction. Follow-ups not yet done: implement it; SURFACE_FITTING.md seeding/BFS/acceptance sections + README CLI docs + TODO.md items still describe the old pipeline. Memory entries below from 2026-07-15/16 largely describe the ABANDONED pipeline (absorb, --skip-stage, seeding masks) — still accurate about the code on disk until the migration lands, and the OCCT gotchas/stage-3 facts remain fully valid.

Key mechanics: \`Interpolate(periodic=true)\` wants the around-the-band axis as the array's FIRST index (transpose when around=v; orientation flip reverses the 2nd index XOR transpose) with the closing station OMITTED (OCCT appends the wrap itself); closure is C1 not C2 (D2 jumps — fine, nothing needs C2); \`SetUKnots\` rescale [0,1]→[0,2π] is exact and makes stage 3.4's 2π-hard-coded wrapped-face machinery apply verbatim once \`BSplineHypothesis.u_periodic\` → chart \`u_periodic()\`; chart returns principal U (rem_euclid 2π), skips the \`home\` U-branch resolution when periodic; U=0 iso IS the cut path (chord params start at first row) so the seam gauge is free and plan_seam_anchors' vertex search is never consulted. 141	  [10.7,15.1,19.7] (axial station +16.4), but its two closed-loop edges —
142	  25777 (f5841,f9597) and 25778 (f5841,f9598) — sit at stations +28.5 and
143	  -14.7, all three ON the cylinder (radial 0.19-0.205mm), 43mm apart along
144	  one line. Faces 9597/9598 are single-FACET islands (1 edge, 1 neighbor,
145	  0 vertices — selection step-4 single-face-planar fallbacks) surrounded
146	  by region 5841. Their edge curves are full 2π IntSS circles (param spans
147	  6.2832) that passed chain validation vacuously: the feature's whole
148	  diameter (0.41mm) is about equal to surface_tolerance (0.4mm), so ANY
149	  small chain near a station is "within tolerance" of the full ring. Two
150	  candidate root causes, possibly combined: (a) genuine disconnection —
151	  BFS regions are connected at deduction, but stage 2.6 greedy selection
152	  keeps only a winner's still-unassigned faces (select_surfaces,
153	  src/stage2/mod.rs ~line 700) and overwrites hyp.faces with that remnant,
154	  with NO connectivity recheck (refine_boundary_assignments can likewise
155	  sever 1-face-wide strips; absorb preserves adjacency per merge but
156	  propagates existing disconnection); (b) sub-tolerance feature scale —
157	  fit-based growth and chain validation are both untethered when the
158	  feature is smaller than surface_tolerance, so contractible island
159	  junctions got full-circle curves that classify as winding in UV. Next

164	  the seam planner to N winding loops is the wrong fix (≥3 winding loops

168	  whose trimmed curves collapse to a point fall back to straight vertex-to-

170	  edge curves come from mesh-boundary-chain fits because surface-surface
171	  intersection failed.

175	  all 196393 mesh faces covered by 41324 planar faces, 41324/41324 OCCT faces

179	  \`U1 == U2\` when both edge endpoints projected to the same curve parameter;
180	  now Errs into the mesh-boundary-chain fallback, matching

182	  ~712) rejects a refined corner position landing within

184	  position (vertex keeps the raw mesh position) — cyclic projection had

188	  failed. NOT a mesh-weld gap: weld is grid+27-neighbor tolerance-based and

1071	  height-field fitted to vertex *positions* over a PCA frame of the
1072	  own+1-ring point cloud, anchored on the face's own vertices (weight
1073	  50), no outlier rejection, inside a hysteresis fixpoint. On clean

1169	   deletion pass (\`bfs.rs\`, \`stage2_bfs_pipeline\`, \`--skip-stage\`,
1170	   greedy selection, absorb, MeshFace hypothesis fields), and re-enable
1171	   the corpus per Rewrite Roadmap step 6.
1172	4. Useful diagnostics while working: \`cargo build --bin dump_regions\`
1173	   (modes: summary, singletons, adj, probe, geom, leaks, rdesc, jet);
1174	   \`--viz-stages=2.1,2.2\` shows sharp (red) / jump (orange) edges and
1175	   region colors.
1176	
1177	### Resolution (2026-07-20): pivot decided
1178	
1179	The "build 2.3/2.4 next as the make-or-break test" recommendation above
1180	was overtaken by evidence. Direct measurement with \`dump_regions\` on the
1181	corpus:
1182	
1183	- **nosecone_medium**: the sphere is ONE clean 644-face region reading
1184	  σ = (0.080, 0.080) = 1/12.5 — and the cone is **129 fragments whose
1185	  descriptors show a clean gradient**: (0.00, 0.00) at the wide base →
1186	  (0.02, 0) mid-cone → (0.08, 0) near the tip. The estimator measures
1187	  correctly — κ_max = 1/ρ(h) genuinely varies along a cone — and a
1188	  threshold on first differences of curvature fires on real gradients. A
1189	  false-positive *class*, not a bug.
1190	- The same statistic must stay blind at 0.35 to those gradient steps
1191	  while firing at 0.33 for pipe_elbow's genuine torus↔cylinder junction:
1192	  **the two classes overlap in the decision statistic. No threshold
1193	  exists**; calibration cannot fix a statistic that does not separate the
1194	  classes.
1195	- The hysteresis fixpoint hits its 10-round cap (oscillates) on every
1196	  fragmenting model (nosecone 130 regions, cone_truncated 14,
1197	  filleted_pipe 94 regions / 670 jump edges); it converges only where
1198	  jump = 0.
1199	- Five estimator variants, each fixing one corpus class and breaking
1200	  another, is the signature of a signal that is not in the local data at
1201	  junction-row scale. Meanwhile everything decided by SHARP edges alone
1202	  was exact — one round, zero new knobs.
1203	
1204	**Decision: scoped pivot** — the architecture in §2 above (second
1205	revision). What carries forward: the sharp-edge partition, region
1206	extraction, the jet estimator (demoted to the §2.1 effort-allocation
1207	prior), viz, \`dump_regions\`, the segmentation tests, and the invariants.
1208	What changed: tangent-junction discovery moved to fit competition — the
1209	former "Contingency: Seed-First Multi-Type Competition" section was
1210	**promoted into §2.3(b)–(c)** (its standalone section is dissolved; its
1211	sub-region-lemma soundness argument, claim semantics, and the
1212	degenerate-limit growth model all live there now), scoped inside smooth
1213	regions, which fixes its two stated cost concerns (bounded arena, no
1214	cross-sharp-edge accidents). The curvature-jump classifier moved to
1215	Abandoned Approaches. Deciding rationale: in every observed failure mode,
1216	the recovery that works is fit validation at vertex tolerance — curvature
1217	is reliable only where dihedral angles already decide (sharp edges) or in
1218	roles where errors cost time instead of correctness (the prior).
1219	
1220	---
1221	
1222	## Abandoned Approaches
1223	
1224	Retired with the 2026-07-17 stage-2 redesign and the 2026-07-20 fit-driven
1225	pivot. Recorded here because each worked well enough to ship models — but
```

## The concrete next steps

**1. Step 6 — Freeform rewiring (`src/stage2/bspline.rs`).** This is the last *algorithmic* piece. The old §2.7 NURBS fallback gathered leftover single-face-planar selections from the now-deleted BFS/selection path; it needs to be rewired to consume the **leftover components** the new competition (`compete.rs`) hands back, with an **island-dissolution guard** so a stray analytic fit stranded among per-facet planes doesn't reconstruct into invalid wires (same failure class the `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX=8` fallback guards against in step 4).
- **Acceptance:** the `tests/nurbs` suite goes green (bspline_hill, hill_with_hole, saddle, arch, elliptical_cylinder, etc.).

**2. Step 7 — Deletion pass + corpus re-enable.** Once freeform lands, delete the nerfed old machinery (`bfs.rs`, `stage2_bfs_pipeline`, old greedy selection/absorb/prune, `--skip-stage`, the `MeshFace` hypothesis fields) and re-pin the stage-2/3/4 integration tests to the new pipeline. Final selection counts are expected to match the old ones (cube = 6 planar, block_with_hole = 6 + 1 cyl, …). Re-enable in the documented order: sharp-edged ccad → analytic primitives → fillet/tangent → nurbs → nosecone(_truncated) → pipe_elbow → 3DBenchy. This is what turns the whole suite green again — right now the old integration tests are **red by design**.

1884	    if config.verbosity >= 1 {

1892	        edge_classes,

1896	    };
1897	    if config.viz_active(2, 1) {

1900	            if matches!(vs.show_and_wait(overlay), VizAction::Quit) {
1901	                return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));
1902	            }
1903	        }
1904	    }
1905	    if !config.stage.at_least(2, 2) {
1906	        return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));

1918	            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
1919	            region_list.len(),
1920	            mesh.faces.len(),

2018	const SHARP_EDGE_COLOR: [f32; 4] = [0.9, 0.1, 0.1, 1.0];
2019	
2020	/// Boundary-edge line segments for viz, one segment per undirected sharp
2021	/// edge, offset slightly along the adjacent faces' average normal so they
2022	/// sit visibly on top of the rendered mesh instead of z-fighting it.
2023	/// Returns positions as [p0, p1, p0, p1, ...] pairs.
2024	fn boundary_line_positions(
2025	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
2026	    ec: &EdgeClassification,
2027	) -> Vec<[f32; 3]> {
2028	    let mut min = [f64::INFINITY; 3];
2029	    let mut max = [f64::NEG_INFINITY; 3];
2030	    for v in &mesh.vertices {
2031	        for (k, c) in [v.x, v.y, v.z].into_iter().enumerate() {
2032	            min[k] = min[k].min(c);
2033	            max[k] = max[k].max(c);
2034	        }
2035	    }
2036	    let diag = ((max[0] - min[0]).powi(2)
2037	        + (max[1] - min[1]).powi(2)
2038	        + (max[2] - min[2]).powi(2))
2039	    .sqrt();
2040	    let offset = if diag.is_finite() { diag * 5e-4 } else { 0.0 };
2041	
2042	    let mut sharp = Vec::new();
2043	    for fi in 0..mesh.faces.len() {
2044	        let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
2045	        for e in 0..vc {
2046	            let ni = mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[e];
2047	            if ni < fi as i32 {
2048	                continue; // open edge, or emitted from the other side
2049	            }
2050	            if ec.class[fi][e] != EdgeClass::Sharp {
2051	                continue;
2052	            }
2053	            let n1 = mesh.faces[fi].normal.unwrap_or([0.0; 3]);
2054	            let n2 = mesh.faces[ni as usize].normal.unwrap_or(n1);
2055	            let mut nn = [n1[0] + n2[0], n1[1] + n2[1], n1[2] + n2[2]];
2056	            let len = (nn[0] * nn[0] + nn[1] * nn[1] + nn[2] * nn[2]).sqrt();
2057	            if len > 1e-12 {
2058	                for c in &mut nn {
2059	                    *c /= len;
2060	                }
2061	            } else {
2062	                nn = [0.0; 3];
2063	            }
2064	            let va = &mesh.vertices[mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[e]];
2065	            let vb = &mesh.vertices[mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[(e + 1) % vc]];
2066	            let pa = [
2067	                (va.x + nn[0] * offset) as f32,
2068	                (va.y + nn[1] * offset) as f32,
2069	                (va.z + nn[2] * offset) as f32,
2070	            ];
2071	            let pb = [
2072	                (vb.x + nn[0] * offset) as f32,
2073	                (vb.y + nn[1] * offset) as f32,
2074	                (vb.z + nn[2] * offset) as f32,
2075	            ];
2076	            sharp.push(pa);
2077	            sharp.push(pb);
2078	        }
2079	    }
2080	    sharp
2081	}
2082	
2083	fn push_boundary_lines(overlay: &mut viz::VizOverlay, mesh: &ConnectedMesh, ec: &EdgeClassification) {
2084	    let sharp = boundary_line_positions(mesh, ec);
2085	    if !sharp.is_empty() {
2086	        overlay.lines.push(viz::LineOverlay {
2087	            positions: sharp,
2088	            color: SHARP_EDGE_COLOR,
2089	            no_depth_test: false,
2090	        });
2091	    }
2092	}
2093	
2094	fn segmentation_edges_overlay(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, ec: &EdgeClassification) -> viz::VizOverlay {
2095	    let mut overlay = viz::VizOverlay::new();
2096	    overlay.status_text = format!(
2097	        "Stage 2.1: sharp-edge classification — {} sharp (red), {} interior [space=continue, q=quit]",
2098	        ec.sharp_count, ec.interior_count,
2099	    );
2100	    push_boundary_lines(&mut overlay, mesh, ec);
2101	    overlay
2102	}
2103	
2104	/// Deterministic distinct color for region \`i\`: golden-ratio hue stepping.
2105	fn region_color(i: usize) -> [f32; 4] {
2106	    let h = (i as f64 * 0.618_033_988_749_895).fract() * 6.0;
2107	    let sector = (h.floor() as i32).rem_euclid(6);
2108	    let f = h - h.floor();
2109	    let (s, v) = (0.55, 0.9);
2110	    let p = v * (1.0 - s);
2111	    let q = v * (1.0 - s * f);
2112	    let t = v * (1.0 - s * (1.0 - f));
2113	    let (r, g, b) = match sector {
2114	        0 => (v, t, p),
2115	        1 => (q, v, p),
2116	        2 => (p, v, t),
2117	        3 => (p, q, v),
2118	        4 => (t, p, v),
2119	        _ => (v, p, q),
2120	    };
2121	    [r as f32, g as f32, b as f32, 1.0]
2122	}
2123	
2124	fn segmentation_regions_overlay(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, seg: &Segmentation) -> viz::VizOverlay {
2125	    let mut overlay = viz::VizOverlay::new();
2126	    let single = seg.regions.iter().filter(|r| r.faces.len() == 1).count();
2127	    let largest = seg.regions.iter().map(|r| r.faces.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
2128	    overlay.status_text = format!(
2129	        "Stage 2.2: {} regions ({} single-face, largest {} faces) — sharp boundaries in red [space=continue, q=quit]",
2130	        seg.regions.len(),
2131	        single,
2132	        largest,
2133	    );
2134	    overlay.face_highlights = seg
2135	        .regions
2136	        .iter()
2137	        .enumerate()
2138	        .map(|(i, r)| viz::FaceHighlight {
2139	            face_indices: r.faces.clone(),
2140	            color: region_color(i),
2141	        })
2142	        .collect();
```

1	//! Stage 2.3(a): the whole-region DOF ladder.
2	//!
3	//! Try to explain each smooth region (stage 2.2) with ONE surface, ascending
4	//! degrees of freedom; the first fit that validates wins. Simplest-first
5	//! resolves degeneracy (any plane also lies on an infinite-radius cylinder; a
6	//! single facet is exactly a plane) without coverage heuristics.
7	//!
8	//! | Rung | Surface  | DOF |
9	//! |------|----------|-----|
10	//! | 1    | Plane    | 3   |
11	//! | 2    | Sphere   | 4   |
12	//! | 3    | Conical  | 5/6 | (cylinder-or-cone; the fitter prefers cylinder)
13	//! | 4    | Torus    | 7   |
14	//!
15	//! The conical rung fits a cylinder first and only falls to a cone when the
16	//! cylinder does not validate (\`ConicalSurface::fit_surface\`), so it covers
17	//! both the 5-DOF cylinder and 6-DOF cone cases in DOF order.
18	//!
19	//! **Validation decides all geometry** (Design Principle #1): every claimed
20	//! vertex within \`--vertex-tolerance\`, every claimed face centroid within
21	//! \`--surface-tolerance\`, and orientation coherence (every face normal
22	//! agrees in sign with the surface normal at its centroid — the per-type
23	//! \`convexity_check\`, which also fixes the hypothesis \`convex\` flag). The
24	//! fitters apply their own numeric-validity guards (cone half-angle bounds,
25	//! torus \`0 < r ≤ R\`, sphere radius cap) and return \`None\` outside them.
26	//!
27	//! A region the ladder cannot explain with one surface is a tangent chain
28	//! (pill capsule, rounded-cube shell, pipe-elbow tube); it goes to stage
29	//! 2.3(b)/(c) scoped competition (\`compete.rs\`). Whatever competition leaves
30	//! unclaimed **terminally degrades to per-facet planes** — total coverage
31	//! can never fail, and a single triangle is exactly a plane.

51	/// Regions larger than this many faces are fitted on a deterministic
52	/// stride subsample (validated against the whole region, refit-augmented on
53	/// a near-miss). Bounds fit cost on huge regions — which, lacking scoped
54	/// competition, are the tangent chains that fail the ladder anyway.
55	const MAX_FIT_FACES: usize = 3000;
56	
57	/// Bounded refit rounds: after a subsample fit, the worst validation
58	/// offenders are added to the fit set and the surface is refit, so a large
59	/// clean surface still validates end to end.
60	const MAX_REFIT: usize = 3;
61	
62	/// How many worst-offender faces to fold into the fit set per refit round.
63	const REFIT_ADD: usize = 32;
64	
65	/// Give-up threshold for a refit near-miss: if the worst offender exceeds

73	/// sub-surface is many more.
74	const LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX: usize = 8;

127	/// Run the whole-region ladder over every smooth region; regions the ladder
128	/// cannot explain go to scoped competition (2.3(b)), and whatever competition
129	/// leaves unclaimed degrades to per-facet planes.
130	pub(super) fn classify_regions(
131	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
132	    regions: &[Region],
133	    jet: &[JetField],
134	    config: &Config,
135	) -> Classification {
136	    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
137	        .faces
138	        .iter()
139	        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
140	        .collect();
141	
142	    let mut cls = Classification::new();
143	    let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
144	    let mut competed_regions = 0usize;
145	    let mut competed_surfaces = 0usize;
146	    let mut facet_faces = 0usize;
147	    for region in regions {
148	        if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
149	            continue;
150	        }
151	        // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
152	        competed_regions += 1;
153	        let (hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);
154	
155	        // A whole undecomposed sub-surface left as per-facet planes (e.g. a
156	        // coarse fillet competition cannot fit) reconstructs to invalid wires
157	        // where it abuts a committed analytic surface — worse than a clean
158	        // polyhedron. When the largest leftover blob is more than a junction
159	        // sliver, discard competition and per-facet the whole region; a clean
160	        // polyhedron is the safe fallback until freeform fitting (2.3(e))
161	        // gives those blobs a principled home. (Healthy competition leaves
162	        // only tiny scattered slivers at junctions.)
163	        if largest_component(mesh, &leftovers) > LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX {
164	            for &fi in &region.faces {
165	                cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
166	            }
167	            facet_faces += region.faces.len();
168	            continue;
169	        }
170	
171	        competed_surfaces += hyps.len();
172	        for hyp in hyps {
173	            cls.push_compete(hyp);
174	        }
175	        // Whatever competition left unclaimed → per-facet planes (total
176	        // coverage can never fail; a single triangle is exactly a plane).
177	        facet_faces += leftovers.len();
178	        for fi in leftovers {
179	            cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
180	        }
181	    }
182	
183	    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
184	        eprintln!(
185	            "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; competition: {} region(s) → {} surfaces + {} per-facet",
186	            ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3],
187	            competed_regions, competed_surfaces, facet_faces,
188	        );
189	    }
190	    cls
191	}
192	
193	/// Try the DOF ladder on one region; commit the first surface that validates.
194	/// Returns true on a committed surface, false if the region fell through
195	/// (caller applies the per-facet fallback).
196	fn classify_one_region(
197	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
198	    region: &Region,
199	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
200	    config: &Config,
201	    cls: &mut Classification,
202	    wins: &mut [usize; 4],
203	) -> bool {
204	    let faces = &region.faces;
205	    let verts = &region.vertices;
206	
207	    if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
208	        cls.push_planar(hyp);
209	        wins[0] += 1;
210	        return true;
211	    }
212	    if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
213	        cls.push_spherical(hyp);
214	        wins[1] += 1;
215	        return true;
216	    }
217	    if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
218	        cls.push_conical(hyp);
219	        wins[2] += 1;
220	        return true;
221	    }
222	    if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<ToroidalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
223	        cls.push_toroidal(hyp);
224	        wins[3] += 1;
225	        return true;
226	    }
227	    false
228	}
229	
230	/// Fit surface type \`S\` to a region and validate it against the whole region.
231	/// Large regions fit on a stride subsample, refit-augmented with the worst
232	/// validation offenders on a near-miss. Returns the committed hypothesis, or
233	/// \`None\` if \`S\` cannot explain the region within tolerance.
234	///
235	/// Also the stage-2.4 merge revalidation entry point (\`repair.rs\`): refit a
236	/// candidate union and confirm it validates end to end before committing.
237	pub(super) fn fit_validate<S: SurfaceType>(
238	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
239	    region_faces: &[usize],
240	    region_verts: &[usize],
241	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
242	    config: &Config,
243	) -> Option<S::Hypothesis> {
244	    let vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
245	    let stol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
246	
247	    // Initial fit set: the whole region, or a deterministic stride subsample
248	    // when the region is large.
249	    let mut fit_faces: Vec<usize> = if region_faces.len() > MAX_FIT_FACES {
250	        let stride = region_faces.len().div_ceil(MAX_FIT_FACES);
251	        region_faces.iter().copied().step_by(stride).collect()
252	    } else {
253	        region_faces.to_vec()
254	    };
255	
256	    for _round in 0..=MAX_REFIT {
257	        let fit_verts = vertices_of(mesh, &fit_faces);
258	        let mut fit = S::fit_surface(mesh, &fit_faces, &fit_verts, config.verbosity)?;
259	        S::determine_convexity(mesh, region_faces, &mut fit);
260	
261	        // Validate every region face; collect near-miss offenders for a refit.
262	        let mut offenders: Vec<(f64, usize)> = Vec::new();
263	        for &fi in region_faces {
264	            // Orientation coherence: a face whose normal disagrees with the
265	            // fitted surface's convexity means the region is not one clean
266	            // surface of type S — no refit rescues that.
267	            if !S::convexity_check(&fit, mesh, fi) {
268	                return None;
269	            }
270	            let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
271	            let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
272	            let mut excess = 0.0_f64;
273	            for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
274	                let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
275	                let d = S::vertex_distance(&fit, &[v.x, v.y, v.z]);
276	                if d > vtol {
277	                    excess = excess.max(d - vtol);
278	                }
279	            }
280	            let cd = S::centroid_distance(&fit, &centroids[fi]);
281	            if cd > stol {
282	                excess = excess.max(cd - stol);
283	            }
284	            if excess > 0.0 {
285	                offenders.push((excess, fi));
286	            }
287	        }
288	
289	        if offenders.is_empty() {
290	            return Some(S::make_hypothesis(
291	                mesh,
292	                region_faces.to_vec(),
293	                region_verts.to_vec(),
294	                &fit,
295	            ));
296	        }
297	
298	        // Near-miss handling: if the worst offender is far out, the region is
299	        // decisively not this surface. Otherwise fold the worst offenders
300	        // into the fit set and refit (bounded rounds).
301	        offenders.sort_by(|a, b| {
302	            b.0.partial_cmp(&a.0)
303	                .unwrap()
304	                .then_with(|| a.1.cmp(&b.1))
305	        });
306	        if offenders[0].0 > REFIT_GIVE_UP * stol {
307	            return None;
308	        }
309	        let before = fit_faces.len();
310	        let existing: HashSet<usize> = fit_faces.iter().copied().collect();
311	        for &(_, fi) in offenders.iter().take(REFIT_ADD) {
312	            if !existing.contains(&fi) {
313	                fit_faces.push(fi);
314	            }
315	        }
316	        fit_faces.sort_unstable();
317	        fit_faces.dedup();
318	        if fit_faces.len() == before {
319	            // No new faces to learn from — cannot improve.
320	            return None;
321	        }
322	    }
323	    None
324	}
325	
326	/// Size of the largest mesh-connected component within a face set.
327	fn largest_component(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> usize {
328	    let member: HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
329	    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
330	    let mut largest = 0usize;
331	    for &start in faces {
332	        if seen.contains(&start) {
333	            continue;
334	        }
335	        let mut size = 0usize;
336	        let mut stack = vec![start];
337	        seen.insert(start);
338	        while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
339	            size += 1;
340	            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
341	            for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
342	                if ni >= 0 {
343	                    let ni = ni as usize;
344	                    if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
345	                        stack.push(ni);
346	                    }
347	                }
348	            }
349	        }
350	        largest = largest.max(size);
351	    }
352	    largest
353	}
354	
355	/// Sorted, deduplicated vertex indices of a face set.
356	fn vertices_of(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<usize> {
357	    let mut v: Vec<usize> = faces
358	        .iter()
359	        .flat_map(|&fi| {
360	            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
361	            mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc].iter().copied()
362	        })
363	        .collect();
364	    v.sort_unstable();
365	    v.dedup();
366	    v
367	}
368	
369	/// A single-face planar hypothesis (the terminal per-facet fallback).
370	fn facet_plane(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> PlanarHypothesis {
371	    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
372	    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
373	    let vertices: Vec<usize> = face.vertex_indices[..vc].to_vec();
374	    let vertex_set: HashSet<usize> = vertices.iter().copied().collect();
375	    let (normal, distance) = fit_plane(&[fi], &vertex_set, &mesh.faces, &mesh.vertices);
376	    let mut error_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
377	    let mut error_min = f64::INFINITY;
378	    let mut error_abs_sum = 0.0_f64;
379	    for &vi in &vertices {
380	        let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
381	        let d = normal[0] * v.x + normal[1] * v.y + normal[2] * v.z - distance;
382	        error_max = error_max.max(d);
383	        error_min = error_min.min(d);
384	        error_abs_sum += d.abs();
385	    }
386	    PlanarHypothesis {
387	        normal,
388	        distance,
389	        faces: vec![fi],
390	        vertices,
391	        error_max,
392	        error_min,
393	        error_abs_sum,
394	    }
395	}
396	
```
</details>

361	#### 2.3 Per-region surface discovery
362	
363	Decompose each smooth region into its true surfaces. Two mechanisms, in
364	order; both decide membership exclusively by fit validation.
365	
366	**Validation (used everywhere, decisive by Design Principle #1):**
367	- Every claimed vertex within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the surface.
368	- Every claimed face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` (chordal
369	  sagitta ≈ h²/8R stays well inside for real tessellations).
370	- Orientation coherence: every face normal agrees in sign with the surface
371	  normal at its centroid — this determines the hypothesis \`convex\` flag and
372	  replaces the old per-face BFS convexity checks.
373	- Numeric-validity guards retained from the fitters (fit-domain
374	  constraints, not selection heuristics): cone half-angle in [2°, 85°],
375	  torus \`0 < r ≤ R\`, sphere radius ≤ 1000× bounding-box diagonal.
376	- **Sub-tolerance features**: when the fitted curvature radius is below
377	  \`--surface-tolerance\` (3DBenchy has r=0.2mm cylinders against the 0.4mm
378	  default), centroid checks are vacuous at that scale; validate by vertex
379	  tolerance alone and mark the region (stage 3's chain validation shares
380	  the blind spot — see TODO, face-5841 family).
381	
382	**(a) Whole-region ladder.** Try to explain the ENTIRE region with one
383	surface, ascending DOF; the first fit that validates wins. Simplest-first
384	resolves degeneracy (any plane also lies on an infinite-radius cylinder; a
385	single facet is exactly a plane) without coverage heuristics.
386	
387	| Rung | Surface | DOF | Fit (see SURFACE_FITTING.md) |
388	|------|---------|-----|------------------------------|
389	| 1 | Plane | 3 | Area-weighted normal average |
390	| 2 | Sphere | 4 | Algebraic LSQ + Gauss-Newton |
391	| 3 | Cylinder | 5 | Normal-covariance axis + 2D circle fit + LM |
392	| 4 | Cone | 6 | Two-path (pointed/truncated) + LM; the existing code fits cylinder and cone together and this rung keeps that dual-fit internally, preferring the cylinder when both validate |
393	| 5 | Torus | 7 | Medial-axis tube-center + SOR axis + LM |
394	
395	The **jet signature orders and prunes the rungs** (a (κ,κ) region tries
396	sphere first; (0,0) tries plane; a failed prediction falls through to the
397	remaining rungs — a misprediction costs one cheap failed fit, never a
398	wrong region). Fits see the whole region — maximal support, which keeps
399	the strip problem dead for any region that IS one surface. For large
400	regions, fit on a deterministic vertex subsample, validate against all
401	vertices, refit augmented with the worst offenders on a near-miss
402	(bounded retries). Most regions of most models end here.
403	
404	**(b) Scoped fit competition** — for regions the ladder cannot explain
405	(tangent chains). Seed-first multi-type competition, run INSIDE the smooth
406	region; the sharp boundary cordons the arena, so competition cost is
407	bounded and cross-region accidents are impossible.
408	
409	- **Seeds**: faces not yet claimed by any committed hypothesis, in
410	  jet-interior-first order (faces whose (κ1,κ2) best match their
411	  neighbors' — the descendant of curvature-guided seed ordering;
412	  misordering costs only time). Claimed faces never seed.
413	- **Per seed**: run the surface types' fit-and-expand from that same seed
414	  (jet-pruned rung set, as in (a)) and commit the hypothesis with the
415	  **largest validated area** (simplest-DOF tiebreak — a plane ties an
416	  infinite-radius cylinder). Initial parameters come from the jet:
417	  r ≈ 1/κ1, sphere center ≈ centroid − n̂/κ1, ruling/axis from the κ≈0
418	  principal direction, torus tube centers directly from medial points —
419	  which also repairs a known weakness of the medial-axis torus fitter on
420	  strip meshes (its pairwise normal-line intersections must reject
421	  near-parallel normal pairs, |n₁·n₂| > 0.95 — exactly what strips are
422	  made of; jet κ1 needs no pairing). Expect the multi-axis LM rescue
423	  ladders to become mostly dead — delete them only once the corpus proves
424	  it.
425	- **Expansion**: BFS from the seed; accept a face when the current fit
426	  validates it (vertex tolerance + orientation coherence). Jet-informed
427	  candidate ordering grows the clean core first, so refits always run
428	  from well-conditioned support and junction rows arrive last, when the
429	  model is already determined.
430	- **Claims**: expansion may grow through faces claimed by earlier
431	  hypotheses — junction-row faces whose vertices genuinely lie on two
432	  surfaces end up claimed by both, which is the true ambiguity; 2.4
433	  resolves multi-claims by best fit.
434	- **Soundness** (the sub-region lemma, under exactness): an accidental fit
435	  is by definition supported on a *sub-region*; the true surface fitted
436	  from an interior seed expands — with vertex tolerance as the arbiter —
437	  to its entire true extent. When all types compete from the SAME seed,
438	  largest-validated-area selects the true surface. Cross-surface leakage
439	  is bounded to genuine junction rows: two distinct analytic surfaces
440	  agreeing to vertex tolerance over an extended patch would be
441	  near-identical. (The abandoned BFS pipeline compared hypotheses born
442	  from *different* seeds at *different* times — that is why commit order
443	  mattered there and does not matter here.)
444	
445	**(c) Growth & model promotion — the degenerate-limit growth model.**
446	Strip-tessellated curved sweeps (the pipe-elbow bend; extrusions) defeat
447	seed-local fits: a cylinder fit claims one strip and stalls, because the
448	neighboring strip sits ~R·Δφ²/2 off the fit — hundreds to thousands of
449	times vertex tolerance. The cure is growing with a model family whose
450	degenerate cases are *regular interior points* of the parameter space:
451	
452	- Parameterize the bent tube by a point on the spine, spine direction,
453	  tube radius \`r\`, spine **curvature** \`k = 1/R\`, and a bending-plane
454	  azimuth — \`k = 0\` IS the cylinder, no infinity anywhere (the
455	  Pratt/Taubin move that makes lines a regular case of circles).
456	- **Promotion trigger — jet-informed, not blanket**: when growth stalls,
457	  read the stalled frontier's jet. Tube-continuation signature (κ1 ≈ the
458	  claimed tube's 1/r, small nonzero κ2) → attempt the promotion refit,
459	  seeding \`k\` from a circle/line fit through the **medial points** of the
460	  claimed support. Incompatible signature ((0,0) plane, (κ,κ) sphere) →
461	  stop; don't spend the refit. This replaces both the old 10×
462	  \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER\` window (far too tight for strips) and blanket
463	  chordal-proximity refitting (too expensive).
464	- **Regularization**: while support spans one strip, \`k\` is
465	  unidentifiable — ridge toward \`k = 0\`, relaxing as support grows.
466	  Exactness bounds the over-flexibility risk: flexibility cannot absorb
467	  faces of a genuinely different surface, because vertex tolerance
468	  remains the acceptance bar.
469	- Once enough arc is claimed, hand off to the full torus fitter and
470	  validate normally. **Model reduction at commit**: collapse \`k ≈ 0\`
471	  (spine deviation from straight < vertex tolerance over the claimed
472	  extent) back to a cylinder.
473	- Scope guard: a bent-tube-only generalization could lock onto a sphere's
474	  latitude band and stall (the dual pathology). The sphere rung competing
475	  from the same seed covers spheres; if band-locking is ever observed
476	  *winning* a commit, the answer is widening the promotion family (the
477	  SOR-profile / Dupin-cyclide direction in SURFACE_FITTING.md), never a
478	  mask.
479	
480	**(d) Model-conditioned jet bands** (effort gates only). With a hypothesis
481	in hand, the model PREDICTS each candidate face's (κ1,κ2) — *including
482	gradients*: a fitted cone predicts (cos α/ρ(h), 0) with ρ varying along
483	the axis, so the gradient class that broke the fit-free classifier is a
484	non-issue against a model. Generous bands (≈[⅓×, 3×] of predicted — jet
485	interiors are few-% accurate, junction rows off by up to ~half) may skip
486	hopeless refit attempts and order candidates. NEVER to reject a face that
487	vertex-validates. (This is the old pipeline's one genuinely effective
488	consistency heuristic — the |n·axis| = sin α normal-axis check — reborn
489	in curvature space with a model to condition on.)
490	
491	**(e) Freeform leftovers.** Connected components of a smooth region left
492	unclaimed after competition (nothing analytic explains them) go to the
493	freeform rung below. Before fitting, **dissolve accidental analytic
494	islands**: a committed analytic hypothesis that is small (≲16 faces) and
495	enclosed by the leftover component is provisionally dissolved into it; if
496	the B-spline fit then validates the whole component *including* the
497	island's vertices, the island stays freeform, else it is restored. This is
498	the old NURBS gathering's confetti-absorption guard reborn with a
499	fit-validated backstop instead of a chordal one. Components below 4 faces,
500	and anything freeform fitting rejects at every grid density, terminally
501	degrade to per-facet planes — total coverage can never fail.
502	
503	**Output**: one hypothesis per committed structure, appended to the typed
504	vectors in \`Stage2Output\` (\`planar_hypotheses\`, \`conical_hypotheses\` —
505	cylinders are cones with \`half_angle == 0\` — \`spherical_hypotheses\`,
506	\`toroidal_hypotheses\`, \`bspline_hypotheses\`). Hypothesis structs keep
507	their existing fields: type-specific parameters, \`faces\`, \`vertices\`,
508	\`convex\`, and error statistics (\`error_max\`, \`centroid_error_max\`).
509	
510	##### Freeform (B-spline) fitting
511	
512	Entered for the unclaimed leftover components of 2.3(e) — parts of a
513	smooth region that neither the whole-region ladder nor competition
514	explains analytically, after the accidental-island dissolution guard. The
515	fitting machinery carries over intact from the previous architecture
516	(implemented 2026-07-10 through 2026-07-15, \`src/stage2/bspline.rs\`); what
517	is deleted is its old region-*gathering* front end (see Abandoned
518	Approaches) — the smooth-region partition and competition leftovers now
519	supply the input. A planar margin of a freeform surface is smoothly
520	connected to it and lands in the same smooth region automatically, which
521	is what the old "tangent-plane merge" step existed to fix (competition's
522	plane rung may claim such a margin; the island-dissolution guard decides,
523	fit-validated, whether it rejoins the freeform patch).
524	
525	**Algorithm (\`deduce_bspline_surfaces\`, per component):**
526	1. Components planar to fitting exactness (10× vertex tolerance,
527	   NaN-guarded: the area-weighted normal of a closed region cancels to
528	   zero) become a multi-face planar hypothesis instead — a plane is
529	   preferable to a B-spline that overfits noise. Components below 4 faces
530	   are not fitted (they degrade to per-facet planes).
531	2. **Parameterize** the region onto the unit square: outer boundary loop pinned by cumulative chord length, 4 corners chosen where the adjacent *outside* region changes (arc-length-quartile fallback when the boundary offers ≠ 4 transitions), interior and hole-loop vertices solved with Floater mean-value weights by Gauss–Seidel (convergence enforced — an unconverged map voids Floater's bijectivity guarantee). Quads are split along the v0–v2 diagonal for weighting and sampling. **Closed bands** (a second boundary loop of extent ≥ half the largest — a small hole floats freely, a comparable second loop means the region wraps, e.g. the wall of a closed prism) are first **seam-cut** into a disk along a shortest vertex path between the two loops (\`cut_region_seam\`): the path follows FACE perimeter edges only (a path along a quad's split diagonal would tear that quad across the cut), its vertices are duplicated, right-side triangle fans switch to the clones, and the boundary re-chains into one loop whose two cut copies carry a \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` id — corner detection then lands the 4 corners exactly at the cut endpoints. The clones keep their originals' positions, so the two parameter-extreme grid columns of the around-the-band axis carry identical data (the periodic fit drops the closing one; the clamped fallback fit keeps both and closes exactly through the shared column).
532	3. **Resample a grid** through the flattened triangulation (uniform-bin point location; numeric misses near corner slivers are clamped to the nearest triangle within one bin — feeding them to the hole membrane extrapolates garbage). Grid stations per axis are the boundary vertices' own pinned positions (stations *between* the vertices of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the fit to the chordal prism — a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own chordal error), subdivided up to an interior-density target of ~1.5·√(region vertices) (a flat margin tessellates coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on a 946-vertex region). Station spacing is **conditioned in 3D-arc measure** (\`axis_stations\`): chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation amplifies data noise by roughly the square of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio, so a boundary mixing fine facets with one long straight chord (the xyz-cube letter walls: a 21× jump) turned a 0.02 mm-flat ribbon into control points 150 mm off the part while passing through every grid node exactly. Near-coincident stations (< 0.25× the median arc gap — the near-singular end of the same conditioning problem) are merged, and segments are subdivided to a fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor segment's pieces, uniformly in arc rather than UV (the Floater map compresses high-curvature areas). Surviving boundary-vertex stations keep their exact pinned positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat and mid-segment samples lie on the true surface. Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`.
533	4. **Fit**: \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate\` (chord-length, C2 cubic) first — the grid is sampled from an exact-on-surface mesh, and OCCT's least-squares approximator reports \`IsDone\` while overshooting its \`Tol3D\` by millimeters near strongly curved patch boundaries. The approximator (tol = surface/8, then surface/2) remains as fallback, and grid density retries at caps 121/181/241. **Closed bands fit periodically**: \`Interpolate(…, thePeriodic=true)\` with the around-the-band axis as the array's FIRST index (transposed when that axis is v; the outward-orientation flip then reverses the second index and accounts for the transpose's normal negation) and the closing station omitted — OCCT appends the wrap-around copy of the first row itself and calls \`SetUPeriodic()\`. The periodic path is interpolation-only (the approximator binding carries no periodic flag). The resulting closure is C1, not C2 (position and D1 match across the wrap to ~1e-14; D2 jumps — measured), which nothing downstream requires. The fitted around-axis knots are then rescaled to exactly [0, 2π] via \`SetUKnots\` — an exact affine reparameterization (~1e-15 drift, measured) — because stage 3.4's wrapped-face machinery hard-codes that period. The U=0 iso is then exactly the cut path (chord-length parameters start at the first supplied row), a free seam gauge.
534	5. **Validate**: first a **control-point bound check** — every pole must lie within the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal. An ill-conditioned fit passes through every grid node exactly yet swings wildly between them, and the vertex/centroid validation below samples at data points, making it structurally blind to inter-node excursions; the surface lies in the convex hull of its poles, so poles near the data bound the whole surface near the data (the xyz-cube blow-up put poles 22 diagonals out; legit fits, including periodic bands, stay well inside the margin). Then every region vertex and face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fit, each projection seeded from the vertex's own parameterization value (sequential seeding breaks at mesh row wraps, and neither \`NextValueOfUV\` nor \`ValueOfUV\` recovers reliably on many-knot surfaces). On failure at every grid density, the region returns to the 2.4 fallback ladder.
535	6. **Closed-band result**: the periodic fit produces ONE \`BSplineHypothesis\` with \`u_periodic = true\`, reconstructed by stage 3 as one wrapped face with a real seam edge — the form CAD kernels (and the references) write. If the periodic fit fails validation at every grid density, the band retries with the clamped fit and is **seam-split** (\`split_cut_hypothesis\`) into two \`SelectedSurface::BSpline\` halves sharing the fitted surface object; their two cut edges are plain edges that a reference model does not have, so the stage 3.1 edge compare will (correctly) flag them — the fallback trades compare-exactness for robustness and is currently exercised by no model.
536	
537	The \`BSplineHypothesis\` owns the fitted \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` (stage 3 reuses the same object via fresh handles) plus a cached, seed-rechecking \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` projector used by the 2.4 repair operations, \`--compare\`, and stage-3 charts/normals/projections. Stage-3 charts of B-spline faces also carry a parameter-space \`home\` anchor (the projection of the region's mean face centroid): a projection landing on a coincident-boundary curve of a seam-split band (the fallback) is ambiguous between the two parameter extremes (Newton, the gap re-check, and \`ValueOfUV\` all cannot tell — both branches are exact), and the branch nearer the face's own interior wins. On a periodic (u_periodic) chart the U-arm of that resolution is replaced by principal-value reduction to [0, 2π) — the same contract as the analytic periodic charts — and the whole wrapped-face machinery (edge-sample branch unwrap, winding classification, seam planning/anchoring, pcurve shifts) applies verbatim. There is no compare exemption: the stage 3.1 edge/vertex compare is fully armed for every edge, including the seam (a real feature both the reconstruction and the reference have).
538	
539	**Periodic-band junctions (stage 3.3)**: the band's two cap junctions are closed-loop edges (no vertices) and must carry PERIODIC curves — seam planning re-anchors a closed edge with \`MakeEdge\` over [t, t+period] at a solved seam azimuth, which a closed-but-non-periodic curve refuses. Two mechanisms deliver that (both were needed on elliptical_cylinder): (a) an IntSS curve that comes back closed-but-non-periodic (observed type: \`Geom_TrimmedCurve\`, exact to ~1e-14) is converted via \`GeomConvert::CurveToBSplineCurve\` + \`SetPeriodic\` (exact, measured); (b) an IntSS curve that comes back as a genuine partial arc falls to the boundary-chain repair, whose closed-chain arm interpolates a periodic C2 B-spline through the chain vertices (\`GeomAPI_Interpolate\` with the periodic flag, closing point not repeated — ~4e-15 chain deviation observed). Partial-arc detection for closed loops keys on the ENDPOINT GAP, not the parameter span: a closed B-spline junction arrives over an arbitrary range (observed [0,1]), and the old span≠2π test replaced the true junction with a bogus circle (14.8 mm off, 12% volume error).
540	
541	**Periodic closed-band conventions (probed 2026-07-15, \`tmp/probe_periodic.rs\`; confirmed in \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface.cxx\`)**: periodic along U = the array's FIRST index; supply each row once (OCCT appends the wrap-around copy of row 1 itself; a duplicated closing row would make the system singular); the result already has \`SetUPeriodic()\` applied. The around-the-band axis is NOT always U in the Floater square (elliptical_cylinder's cut copies land as the u-extreme sides, making v the around axis): \`cut_around_axis\` detects it from the \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` loop entries, and \`fit_grid\` transposes the array so the surface's U is always the around axis — \`BSplineHypothesis.u_periodic\` is a single flag, no per-axis plumbing. The stage-3.4 wrapped-face machinery (branch unwrap in \`sample_edge_uv\`, winding classification, \`align_loop\` offsets, pcurve shifts, seam handling — \`grep TWO_PI src/stage3.rs\`) hard-codes the 2π period; \`SetUKnots\` rescales the fitted knots exactly, after which it all applies verbatim once the chart reports \`u_periodic()\`. Debug probes: \`BREPPER_DEBUG_EDGE_UV=1\` prints every chart projection during edge sampling; \`--stage=3.4 -v\` prints per-edge pcurve deviations + UV endpoints per face; the \`-vvv\` fit-residual-vs-own-grid probe separates fit error from sampler/projection error.
542	
543	**Implementation notes (from the stage-3 rework, 2026-07-08; all implemented — kept for reference):**
544	- *The chart contract.* A chart provides \`point_uv(p) → (u, v, pole_radius)\` and \`point_at(u, v)\`, plus \`u_periodic()\`/\`v_periodic()\`. For a B-spline chart: \`pole_radius\` is ∞ (like the plane chart — fitted height-field patches have no UV degeneracies), and **both periodic flags must be false for open patches** (\`u_periodic()\` is true only for the periodic closed-band fit). Every mod-2π unwrap/branch-lift in stage 3.4 is gated on those flags; reducing non-periodic parameter coordinates mod 2π silently corrupts them (a real bug on plane charts once, masked for months — see LEARNINGS). With both flags false, the whole seam/winding/degenerate-pole machinery self-disables and B-spline faces take the plain-loop path end to end.
545	- *The chart must wrap the exact \`Handle(Geom_BSplineSurface)\` stored in \`FaceDescriptor.surface\`* — never a reconstruction. Use one \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` per face: \`new_handlegeomsurface\` + \`value_of_uv(&mut self, pnt, precision)\` — note \`&mut\` (it caches internally). For sampling along an edge curve, prefer \`next_value_of_uv(prev_uv, pnt, preci, maxpreci)\`. Validate each solution with \`gap()\` and fall back to a fresh \`value_of_uv\` when the gap exceeds tolerance.
546	- *Every \`match\` on \`SelectedSurface\` needs a \`BSpline\` arm* — \`create_occt_surface\`, \`surface_faces\`, \`surface_normal_at_point\`, \`project_point_to_selected\`, \`build_face_chart\`, the concave-flag matches, the tangent-edge dispatch in 3.3, and the cosmetic \`stype\` labels + viz overlays.
547	- *Fix the fitted patch's normal orientation at fit time.* \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface\` follows the orientation of the input point grid, so build the grid such that d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals — then B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`/FORWARD. Choosing the convention at fit time is one line; recovering it later is a per-face heuristic.
548	- *Edge curves lean on the boundary chain.* \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` is flakier against B-spline surfaces than between quadrics, and tangent-classified junctions (a fitted patch usually meets its neighbors near-tangentially) may have no analytic construction — both routes end at the mesh-boundary-chain machinery (\`repair_edge_curve_from_boundary\`, \`edge_curve_from_boundary_chain\`), which is surface-pair-agnostic and fits line, circle, AND a \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline\` curve through the chain vertices, keeping whichever deviates least.
549	- *What NURBS will not stress — don't build for it:* seams, winding loops, degenerate pole edges, and edge splitting apply only to periodic/wrapped faces; non-periodic B-spline patches never take those paths.
550	- *Test models*: \`tests/nurbs/\` — 8 exact-reference STL/STEP pairs from \`gen-test-models\` (see tests/nurbs/README.md for the difficulty ladder and the strip-mesh rationale: ruled/extruded models ship full-length strips with NO interior vertices along the ruling, exactly what real CAD exporters emit).
551	
552	#### 2.4 Resolution & repair
553	
554	Turns 2.3's claims into a strict, connected, validated partition. All
555	operations are fit-validated and deterministic (fixed iteration order —
556	by region/hypothesis index, smallest region first for merges — bounded
557	rounds).
558	
559	**Claim resolution** — the boundary placement at tangent junctions: faces
560	claimed by multiple hypotheses (junction rows) go to the claimant whose
561	surface fits their vertices best, requiring a **2× margin** to prefer a
562	different side than the larger claimant (a true junction face fits both
563	sides equally — the margin prevents arbitrary flapping; residual ties
564	break to the larger claimant, then lower hypothesis index). Exact vertex
565	distances, no curvature involved.
566	
567	**Boundary relabel** (±1-row polish; port of the old
568	\`refine_boundary_assignments\`): after resolution, walk region-boundary
569	faces; move a face when an adjacent region's surface fits its vertices at
570	least 2× better; iterate to a fixpoint (≤ 10 rounds; boundaries only move
571	locally), then rebuild affected regions' vertex lists. Region *geometry*
572	is not refitted — only the membership map changes.
573	
574	**Merge**: absorb region S into an adjacent larger region T when every
575	vertex of S lies within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of T's fitted surface and
576	every centroid of S within \`--surface-tolerance\`; refit the union,
577	revalidate, keep only if valid. Eats junction-circle singletons (faces
578	whose vertices sit ON a tangent seam — genuinely ambiguous, claimable by
579	either side), chord-degenerate slivers (a single-facet-wide band of a
580	coarse cylinder validates as its own facet plane, but its vertices lie
581	exactly on the neighbor cylinder), and residual same-surface fragments.
582	This is the abandoned absorb pass's *tight* rule reborn with a principled
583	criterion — vertex tolerance, at which "on the same surface" is decisive;
584	never the chordal slop of the abandoned loose rule (0.4mm slop, 794
585	BRepCheck failures on planar-only 3DBenchy). B-spline surfaces are never
586	merge TARGETS for faces outside their fitted region: a fit vouches only
587	for its data (the island-dissolution guard in 2.3(e) is the fit-validated
588	way into a freeform patch).
589	
590	**Connectivity re-split + invariants** (hard assertions before stage 3):
591	resolution and relabeling can disconnect a region — re-split into
592	mesh-connected components (identical fitted geometry on two non-adjacent
593	regions is legal). Then assert: every mesh face belongs to exactly one
594	region (total coverage); every region is mesh-connected; every region's
595	surface validates the region's own vertices/centroids.
596	
597	(The old standalone "split repair" is gone: under-segmentation cannot
598	survive 2.3 — competition decomposes within smooth regions — and a region
599	nothing explains degrades to per-facet planes via 2.3(e). There is no
600	mechanism left that re-runs a curvature classifier at lowered thresholds;
601	that mechanism died with the classifier.)
602	
603	#### 2.5 Selected surfaces & validation
604	
605	- Build \`Vec<SelectedSurface>\` — one per region, the **unchanged stage-3
606	  input contract**: \`surface_type\` (Planar, Cylindrical, Spherical, Conical,
607	  Toroidal, BSpline), \`hypothesis_index\` into the typed vectors, \`faces\`,
608	  \`vertices\`.
609	- Prune hypothesis vectors to selected entries and remap indices (as
610	  before).
611	- Print a summary: surface counts by type, faces covered, repair-operation
612	  counts.
613	- With \`--compare\`: for each selected surface, project each member face's
614	  centroid onto the fitted surface and measure the distance to the nearest
615	  reference STEP surface with \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`; error above
616	  \`--surface-tolerance\`. (Centroid-to-surface distance includes tessellation
617	  sagitta ≈ h²/8R — small relative to \`--surface-tolerance\` for typical
618	  tessellations.) Also re-run the 2.2 partition diagnostics against the
619	  final (post-repair) partition, now as hard checks.
620	
621	#### CLI notes (stage numbering and modes)
622	
623	- \`--stage\` sub-stages: **2.1** edge classification, **2.2** region
624	  extraction, **2.3** classification, **2.4** repair, **2.5** selection.
625	- \`--surface-types=<list>\` (new) restricts the ladder and competition rung
626	  set (default: all). \`--surface-types=planar\` is **exact planar-only
627	  mode**: curved smooth regions fail the one-rung ladder, competition's
628	  plane rung claims only coplanar sets, and the rest degrades to per-facet
629	  planes via the 2.3(e) terminal fallback — the mode that converts
630	  3DBenchy with volume matching the official 15.55 cm³ — without any
631	  chordal-slop absorb by construction.
632	- \`--skip-stage\` is retired with the old stage numbering; its
633	  type-skipping role is \`--surface-types\`, and its absorb/refinement gates
634	  have no equivalent (absorb is gone; repair is core, and debugging-level
635	  disabling of repair operations can be a \`-Z\`-style debug option if ever
636	  needed).
637	
638	#### Implementation hints (read before writing code)
639	
640	Function-level guidance for whoever implements the pivot, written against

1974	/// Stage 2.7 entry: gather connected components of single-face planar
1975	/// selections and fit each with a B-spline surface (or, for regions that are
1976	/// planar to fitting exactness, a multi-face plane). Successful fits replace
1977	/// the component's single-face planar selections in \`selected_surfaces\`.
1978	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1979	pub(super) fn deduce_bspline_surfaces(
1980	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
1981	    selected_surfaces: &mut Vec<SelectedSurface>,
1982	    planar_hypotheses: &mut Vec<PlanarHypothesis>,
1983	    conical_hypotheses: &[ConicalHypothesis],
1984	    spherical_hypotheses: &[SphericalHypothesis],
1985	    toroidal_hypotheses: &[ToroidalHypothesis],
1986	    bspline_hypotheses: &mut Vec<BSplineHypothesis>,
1987	    viz: Option<&VizSender>,
1988	    config: &Config,
1989	) {
1990	    let t = Instant::now();
1991	    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();
1992	
1993	    // Face → selected surface index, and the single-face planar face set.
1994	    let mut face_to_selected = vec![usize::MAX; num_faces];
1995	    for (si, s) in selected_surfaces.iter().enumerate() {
1996	        let faces: &[usize] = match s {
1997	            SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &planar_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1998	            SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &conical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
1999	            SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces,
2000	            SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &toroidal_hypotheses[*i].faces,
2001	            SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &bspline_hypotheses[*i].faces,
2002	        };
2003	        for &fi in faces {
2004	            face_to_selected[fi] = si;
2005	        }
2006	    }
2007	    let selection_faces = |si: usize| -> &[usize] {
2008	        match selected_surfaces[si] {
2009	            SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &planar_hypotheses[i].faces,
2010	            SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &conical_hypotheses[i].faces,
2011	            SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &spherical_hypotheses[i].faces,
2012	            SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &toroidal_hypotheses[i].faces,
2013	            SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &bspline_hypotheses[i].faces,
2014	        }
2015	    };
2016	    let is_single_planar = |si: usize| -> bool {
2017	        matches!(
2018	            selected_surfaces[si],
2019	            SelectedSurface::Planar(pi) if planar_hypotheses[pi].faces.len() == 1
2020	        )
2021	    };
2022	    let single: Vec<bool> = (0..num_faces)
2023	        .map(|fi| face_to_selected[fi] != usize::MAX && is_single_planar(face_to_selected[fi]))
2024	        .collect();
2025	    let candidate: Vec<bool> = (0..num_faces)
2026	        .map(|fi| {
2027	            face_to_selected[fi] != usize::MAX
2028	                && selection_faces(face_to_selected[fi]).len() <= SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES
2029	        })
2030	        .collect();
2031	
2032	    // Connected components over mesh adjacency, lowest face index first.
2033	    // Components grow only across SMOOTH edges: a freeform surface varies
2034	    // by at most the tessellation's angular step between neighbors (the
2035	    // same angular tolerance the analytic BFS gathering uses), while a
2036	    // sharp crease marks a real B-rep edge between distinct surfaces. This
2037	    // keeps legitimately planar orphans (a single-quad bottom face, a 45°
2038	    // chamfer strip) from gluing freeform regions — or entire chamfered
2039	    // cubes — into impossible components.
2040	    let smooth_cos = config.angular_tolerance_rad.min(std::f64::consts::PI).cos();
2041	    let smooth_edge = |a: usize, b: usize| -> bool {
2042	        match (mesh.faces[a].normal, mesh.faces[b].normal) {
2043	            (Some(na), Some(nb)) => {
2044	                na[0] * nb[0] + na[1] * nb[1] + na[2] * nb[2] >= smooth_cos
2045	            }
2046	            _ => false,
2047	        }
2048	    };
2049	    let mut visited = vec![false; num_faces];
2050	    let mut components: Vec<Vec<usize>> = Vec::new();
2051	    // Planes already tangent-merged into an earlier component (a plane
2052	    // must not be consumed by two components).
2053	    let mut merged_planes: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
2054	    for start in 0..num_faces {
2055	        if !candidate[start] || visited[start] {
2056	            continue;
2057	        }
2058	        let mut comp = Vec::new();
2059	        let mut stack = vec![start];
2060	        visited[start] = true;
2061	        while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
2062	            comp.push(fi);
2063	            let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
2064	            for k in 0..face.vertex_count as usize {
2065	                let ni = face.neighbors[k];

2071	                    }
2072	                }
2073	            }
2074	        }
2075	        comp.sort_unstable();
2076	        // A component with no single-face planar member is a real small
2077	        // feature caught by the widened candidate set — leave it.
2078	        let n_single = comp.iter().filter(|&&fi| single[fi]).count();
2079	        if config.verbosity >= 2 {
2080	            eprintln!(
2081	                "  Stage 2.7: component of {} faces ({} single-face planar, {} absorbed)",
2082	                comp.len(),
2083	                n_single,
2084	                comp.len() - n_single,
2085	            );
2086	        }
2087	        if n_single == 0 {
2088	            continue;
2089	        }
2090	        // Small analytic/planar selections are only *evidence of accident*
2091	        // when the unexplained (single-face) area dominates. When absorbed
2092	        // faces would outnumber the single-face leftovers, trust the
2093	        // analytic fits and keep just the leftovers.
2094	        if comp.len() - n_single > n_single {

2098	        // boundary with the component is smooth: a plane tangent to a

> I'm tracing through the old gathering process: it starts by identifying single-face planar selections and candidates, then grows connected components across smooth edges, applies tangent-plane merges, and for each component either fits it to a plane or calls `fit_component`. After replacing consumed selections, the new architecture handles freeform leftovers differently.
> 
> The key is intercepting the unclaimed connected components that currently come from `compete_region` in classify.rs—those are being converted to per-facet planes, but I need to redirect them to the freeform rung instead. I'm looking at how `compete_region` returns those leftovers and what `fit_component` expects from them.

> I'm seeing how `fit_component` works with its parameters — it needs the mesh, the face indices for the component, a mapping that tracks which selected surface is adjacent to each boundary edge (crucial for detecting corners), and a boolean array marking which faces belong to the component. 330	        let l = |g: usize| global_to_local[&g];
331	        tris.push([l(vi[0]), l(vi[1]), l(vi[2])]);
332	        tri_face.push(rf);
333	        if vc == 4 {
334	            tris.push([l(vi[0]), l(vi[2]), l(vi[3])]);
335	            tri_face.push(rf);
336	        }
337	        for k in 0..vc {
338	            let ni = face.neighbors[k];
339	            if ni < 0 || !in_region[ni as usize] {
340	                let a = vi[k];
341	                let b = vi[(k + 1) % vc];
342	                let outside_id = if ni >= 0 {
343	                    face_to_selected[ni as usize]
344	                } else {
345	                    usize::MAX
346	                };
347	                outside.insert((a.min(b), a.max(b)), outside_id);
348	            }
349	        }
350	    }
351	
352	    let mut region = Region {
353	        faces,
354	        tris,
355	        tri_face,
356	        verts,
357	        positions,
358	        global_to_local,
359	        outside,
360	        loops: Vec::new(),
361	    };
362	    region.loops = chain_boundary_loops(&region)?;
363	    if region.loops.is_empty() {
364	        return Err("region has no boundary loop".to_string());
365	    }
366	    Ok(region)
367	}
368

134	/// Decompose a tangent-chain region. Returns the committed analytic
135	/// hypotheses (a strict partition after claim resolution) plus the leftover
136	/// faces no hypothesis explained.
137	pub(super) fn compete_region(
138	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
139	    region: &Region,
140	    jet: &[JetField],
141	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
142	    config: &Config,
143	) -> (Vec<CompeteHyp>, Vec<usize>) {
144	    let n = mesh.faces.len();
145	    let debug = config.verbosity >= 3 || std::env::var("BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE").is_ok();
146	    let mut in_region = vec![false; n];
147	    for &fi in &region.faces {
148	        in_region[fi] = true;
149	    }
150	
151	    // Region curvature scale: a face reads "flat" when its principal
152	    // curvature radius exceeds a couple of region diameters.
153	    let extent = region_extent(mesh, region);
154	    let flat_eps = if extent > 0.0 { 1.0 / (2.0 * extent) } else { 1e-6 };
155	
156	    // Seed order: jet-interior-first (ascending curvature mismatch with
157	    // region neighbors), ties by face index for determinism.
158	    let mut seeds = region.faces.clone();
159	    let score: Vec<f64> = (0..n)
160	        .map(|fi| {
161	            if !in_region[fi] {
162	                return f64::INFINITY;
163	            }
164	            neighbor_jet_mismatch(mesh, fi, jet, &in_region)
165	        })
166	        .collect();
167	    seeds.sort_by(|&a, &b| {
168	        score[a]
169	            .partial_cmp(&score[b])
170	            .unwrap()
171	            .then_with(|| a.cmp(&b))
172	    });
173	
174	    let mut claimed = vec![false; n]; // out of the seed pool once claimed
175	    let mut committed: Vec<CompeteHyp> = Vec::new();
176	    let mut claim_lists: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n]; // face -> committed idxs
177	
178	    for &seed in &seeds {
179	        if claimed[seed] {
180	            continue;
181	        }
182	        let jf = jet[seed];
183	        let a1 = jf.k1.abs();
184	        let a2 = jf.k2.abs();
185	
186	        // Grow the jet-pruned candidate types; keep the largest validated
187	        // area (simplest-DOF tiebreak).
188	        let mut best: Option<(CompeteHyp, f64, u8)> = None; // (hyp, area, dof)
189	        let consider = |hyp_area: Option<(CompeteHyp, f64)>, dof: u8, best: &mut Option<(CompeteHyp, f64, u8)>| {
190	            if let Some((h, area)) = hyp_area {
191	                let better = match best {
192	                    None => true,
193	                    Some((_, ba, bd)) => area > *ba + 1e-9 || ((area - *ba).abs() <= 1e-9 && dof < *bd),
194	                };
195	                if better {
196	                    *best = Some((h, area, dof));
197	                }
198	            }
199	        };
200	
201	        // Plane is always a candidate (cheap; catches misread flats).
202	        let plane = grow::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, 1);
203	        let mut cone_area = None;
204	        let mut sphere_area = None;
205	        let mut torus_area = None;
206	        if a1 >= flat_eps {
207	            // Curved: a developable (cylinder/cone) is always plausible.
208	            let cone = grow::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_CYLINDER_FACES);
209	            cone_area = cone.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
210	
211	            // Toroidal candidate: a direct grow from this seed (fillet tori,
212	            // bend tubes), skipped when the seed reads sphere-like — every
213	            // adjacent quad-row band of a sphere lies near some torus, so a
214	            // (κ,κ) seed's torus is at best a bogus band that loses to the
215	            // sphere anyway. A torus seed reads |κ2| < |κ1| (κ2 varies
216	            // azimuthally around the tube; it only approaches κ1 near the
217	            // degenerate R≈r hole of a donut — a whole-region ladder case,
218	            // not a competition case).
219	            let mut torus = if a2 < 0.8 * a1 {
220	                let patch = seed_patch(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet);
221	                torus_candidate(mesh, &in_region, &patch, &[seed], centroids, config)
222	            } else {
223	                None
224	            };
225	            // Bent-tube promotion: a cylinder that stalled against
226	            // tube-continuation faces is the developable prefix of a bent
227	            // tube; grow a torus from the pure-bend patch past the stall.
228	            if let Some((cyl, _, cyl_faces)) = cone.as_ref() {
229	                if cyl.is_cylinder() {
230	                    let promoted =
231	                        promote_bend(mesh, &in_region, cyl, cyl_faces, jet, centroids, config);
232	                    if let Some(p) = promoted {
233	                        if torus.as_ref().is_none_or(|(_, a, _)| p.1 > *a) {
234	                            torus = Some(p);
235	                        }
236	                    }
237	                }
238	            }
239	            torus_area = torus.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
240	
241	            consider(cone.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Conical(h), a)), 2, &mut best);
242	            // Curved in both directions → also try a sphere.
243	            if a2 >= 0.3 * a1 {
244	                let sphere = grow::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_SPHERE_FACES);
245	                sphere_area = sphere.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
246	                consider(sphere.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Spherical(h), a)), 1, &mut best);
247	            }
248	            consider(torus.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h), a)), 3, &mut best);
249	        }
250	        let plane_area = plane.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
251	        consider(plane.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Planar(h), a)), 0, &mut best);
252	
253	        if debug && best.is_none() {
254	            eprintln!(
255	                "    compete: seed {seed} jet=({:.3},{:.3}) class {:?} → NO GROWTH (plane {plane_area:?} cone {cone_area:?} sphere {sphere_area:?} torus {torus_area:?})",
256	                jf.k1, jf.k2, curvature_class(jf),
257	            );
258	        }
259	
260	        match best {
261	            Some((hyp, area, dof)) => {
262	                if debug {
263	                    let kind = match hyp {
264	                        CompeteHyp::Planar(_) => "plane",
265	                        CompeteHyp::Conical(ref h) => if h.is_cylinder() { "cyl" } else { "cone" },

272	                    );
273	                }
274	                let idx = committed.len();
275	                for &f in hyp.faces() {

279	                committed.push(hyp);
280	            }
281	            None => {
282	                // The seed grows nothing analytic; drop it from the pool so
283	                // the loop terminates. It becomes a leftover.
284	                claimed[seed] = true;
285	            }
286	        }
287	    }
288	
289	    resolve_claims(mesh, &region.faces, &mut committed, &claim_lists)
290	}

293	/// leave the larger claimant), rebuild each hypothesis's face/vertex lists,

295	fn resolve_claims(
296	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
297	    region_faces: &[usize],
298	    committed: &mut Vec<CompeteHyp>,
299	    claim_lists: &[Vec<usize>],
300	) -> (Vec<CompeteHyp>, Vec<usize>) {

302	        .iter()
303	        .map(|h| h.faces().iter().map(|&fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices)).sum())
304	        .collect();
305	
306	    let mut assigned: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); committed.len()];
307	    let mut leftovers: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
308	
309	    for &fi in region_faces {
310	        let claimants = &claim_lists[fi];
311	        if claimants.is_empty() {
312	            leftovers.push(fi);
313	            continue;
314	        }
315	        if claimants.len() == 1 {
316	            assigned[claimants[0]].push(fi);
317	            continue;
318	        }
