> Now I'm thinking through the cleanest way to integrate step 6 with the compete.rs interface. The key design is that freeform operates on connected components of leftover faces within smooth regions, with small analytic islands (≤16 faces) provisionally dissolved. Components with fewer than 4 faces get converted to per-facet planes, and anything freeform can't handle at any grid density falls back to per-facet as well.
> 
> For the test models like bspline_hill and saddle, the whole region is typically one large smooth area. The ladder classification fails since these aren't plane/sphere/cone/torus, so competition runs and seeds grow small accidental analytic patches. The leftover blob ends up large, which currently triggers the LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX discard path that converts everything to per-facet planes. The insight is to hand that large leftover blob to freeform instead of discarding it outright. Then I can run a deduce_bspline_surfaces-style pass over the per-facet result to gather components from single-face planar selections and small analytic patches.
> 
> The pragmatic approach is to add a freeform pass after competition that operates directly on the leftover structure, reusing the existing fit_component and build_region machinery. This new front-end would collect leftover components as connected face groups, run island dissolution against committed analytic hypotheses, call fit_component, and commit BSpline results or fall back to per-facet. The question is whether this pass should run post-hoc over the final classification result or integrate earlier in the pipeline. There's a tension with the LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX threshold: if I discard whole regions per-facet and then re-gather, I risk losing analytic fits in mixed regions (freeform plus real analytic geometry), though the test models appear to be pure freeform anyway, so this might not be a practical concern.
> 
> But there's another wrinkle — running gathering after repair across the whole class would also pick up leftovers from other regions like the coarse-mesh fallback areas, and fitting those into a bspline could either fix a failing model or introduce a new failure, which is risky and needs testing.
> 
> Option 2 is to apply freeform directly where the leftovers are produced, before falling back to per-facet handling. This keeps the approach faithful to the design spec and naturally scopes freeform to just the region that failed the ladder, using that region's own competition results for island dissolution. The tradeoff is more new code and needing to construct the face selection locally, though the design spec suggests planar margins should land in the same smooth region anyway, so cross-region tangent merging shouldn't be an issue.
> 
> Now I'm thinking through whether a planar margin from a freeform patch actually stays within the same smooth region. Since the margin meets the patch tangentially with a smooth edge, it should be in the same region. The competition's plane rung might claim it as a committed analytic plane, and then island dissolution decides whether it rejoins the freeform patch. But island dissolution as currently described only handles small enclosed islands, so I need to check if that covers this case.
> 
> Rather than overthinking the design, let me just build the project and run some actual NURBS models through the new pipeline to see what the classification output looks like. That'll give me the empirical baseline I need before diving deeper into edge cases.
> 
> I'll check the workflow docs and test setup, then kick off a release build. Since there's a prebuilt OCCT symlink available, the build should be fairly quick despite the usual compilation time.

32	The project uses the \`opencascade-sys\` crate (auto-generated Rust FFI bindings to OpenCASCADE Technology) via a path dependency. MANDATORY: All useful C++ OCCT APIs should be available in the binding - if you want to use one that doesn't seem to be bound, complain loudly about it AND STOP.
33	
34	\`\`\`
35	../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys   ← Rust FFI bindings (8700+ types)
36	../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md  ← Naming conventions & usage patterns
37	../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/generated/  ← All generated .rs modules
38	../opencascade-rs/crates/occt-sys/OCCT/src/  ← OCCT C++ source (for reading docs/headers)
39	../opencascade-rs/crates/occt-sys/OCCT/dox/  ← OCCT C++ documentation
40	\`\`\`
41	
42	The \`builtin\` feature (default) compiles OCCT from source — first build takes ~8 minutes. Subsequent builds are fast.
43	
44	## Commit Messages
45	
46	All commit messages must include:
47	
48	1. **A clear description** of the changes made
49	2. **The AI agent/model** used to generate the changes (e.g., "Claude Sonnet 4 (Anthropic, 2025)")
50	3. **The user prompts** that led to the changes
51	
52	Format:
53	\`\`\`
54	<descriptive commit title>
55	
56	<detailed description of changes>
57	
58	---
59	Generated with assistance from <model name> (<provider>, <year>)
60	
61	User prompt(s):
62	- "<prompt 1>"
63	- "<prompt 2>"
64	  (include context for prompts that reference previous options/decisions)
65	\`\`\`
66	
67	## Development Plan Maintenance
68	
69	Keep DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md up to date with each commit:
70	
71	1. **Mark completed items** - Check off (\`[x]\`) any Implementation Phases items that are completed by the commit
72	2. **Add new tasks** - If work reveals new tasks or sub-tasks not in the plan, add them to the appropriate phase
73	3. **Modify existing tasks** - If a planned approach changes (e.g., different algorithm, new dependency), update the relevant sections
74	4. **Include in commit** - Changes to DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md should be part of the same commit as the implementation work
75	
76	## README Maintenance
77	
78	Keep README.md up to date when changes affect user-facing behavior:
79	
80	1. **New dependencies** - Add to the dependencies list with installation instructions
81	2. **New CLI options** - Update the usage examples and options documentation
82	3. **Changed defaults** - Document any changes to default parameter values
83	4. **New features** - Add brief descriptions of significant new capabilities
84	5. **Include in commit** - README updates should be part of the same commit as the feature work
85	
86	### IMPORTANT: Time Builds and Tests
87	
88	**Always** prefix build and test commands with \`time\`. When they take more than 2 minutes ask the user whether to speed them up.
89	
90	Correct: \`time make -j4 2>&1\`
91	Correct: \`time ctest --output-on-failure -j4 2>&1\`
92	
93	## Temporary Test Code
94	
95	When writing temporary code for debugging or testing:
96	
97	1. **Put files in \`tmp/\`** - This directory is in the project root and ignored by git. Don't use \`/tmp\` or other directories outside the workspace.
98	
99	2. **Don't delete temporary code** - Leave it in \`tmp/\` in case it's useful later.
100	
101	## Library Reuse
102	
103	Before implementing non-trivial functionality (text rendering, file parsing, data structures, etc.), search for existing open-source libraries that provide that functionality. Evaluate whether using a library will be better for code simplicity and maintainability than writing it from scratch. Consider factors like: compatibility with existing dependencies, maintenance status, API complexity, and how well the library fits the use case. Then go with your decision — either use the library or implement it yourself — and briefly explain why.
104	
105	## Generating CodeCAD Test Models
106	
107	Test models can be generated with ccad - see \`../codecad/docs\` for documentation. To regenerate the STL and STEP files for CodeCAD-based tests:
108	
109	\`\`\`bash
110	# Requires 'ccad' tool installed (https://codecad.xyz)
111	./tests/ccad/generate_models.sh
112	\`\`\`
113	
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>Edit TODO.md</summary>

**Before:**
```markdown
- **End-cap pcurve degenerates on STEP export, ~14% volume loss
  (`pipe_elbow_10_fine`; found + diagnosed 2026-07-21 during step-5 debug).
  DETECTED (round-trip self-check, stage4.rs) but NOT yet fixed.** After
  stage-2.3(c) torus promotion, `pipe_elbow_10_fine` recognizes the correct
  5-surface topology (1 torus R=10 r=5 + 2 cylinders r=5 + 2 end planes) and
  builds a fully valid in-memory solid; the *written* STEP re-reads 14% low
  (3087 vs 3590 mm³) → fails `--compare` at stage 4.1. `pipe_elbow_10_medium`
  is identical in every analytic parameter and STEP entity count yet
  round-trips faithfully. Deep dive (probe: read the written STEP back and
  report per-face type + `BRepTools::UVBounds` + `BRepCheck`; and an
  in-memory `UVBounds`/`BRepCheck` dump of the compound before the write):
  - **The deduced geometry is CORRECT and IDENTICAL-quality for both.**
    In memory, every face of both meshes has clean, exact pcurve bounds —
    the two end-cap planes are each `dU=dV=10.0000` (a radius-5 disk), the
    torus is `U∈[0,π/2] V∈[0,2π]`, correct `BRepGProp` volume 3589.98 mm³,
    and BOTH pass geometric AND topological `BRepCheck_Analyzer`. There is
    no in-memory defect to find.
  - **The corruption is entirely in OCCT's STEP export.** OCCT re-expresses
    every pcurve as a B-spline on write (the file has 20 `B_SPLINE_CURVE`s,
    only 3 exact `CIRCLE`s). For the fine mesh this conversion produces a
    DEGENERATE pcurve for one end-cap plane: on re-import that face's UV
    domain collapses to a single point (`U=[59.14681,59.14681] V≈[0,0]`)
    while its 3D area stays ~78.5. The re-imported solid is STILL
    topologically valid and closed (1 solid, 1 shell, 5 faces, 14 edges,
    `BRepCheck` valid) — but `BRepGProp`'s volume integral, which uses the
    collapsed pcurve domain, under-counts by one end-cap's worth (~500 mm³).
  - **Likely trigger** (lead for the fix): the fine mesh's periodic faces
    carry a sub-micron parametric seam offset — torus `V=[-3.5e-6, 2π-3.5e-6]`
    and a cylinder `U=[-1e-5, 2π-1e-5]`, versus medium's exact `[0, 2π]`.
    That offset (from fitting pcurves/edges to the 4×-denser, differently
    positioned vertex chains) is invisible to `BRepCheck` but appears to be
    what tips OCCT's pcurve→B-spline export into the degenerate case for the
    adjacent end-cap. The fix probably lives in stage-3 pcurve/seam
    construction (`build_pcurve`, seam re-anchoring): snap periodic-face
    parameter domains to exactly `[0, 2π]` and/or attach exact-circle
    pcurves (not B-splines) for circular edges so OCCT does not resample.
  Reproduce: `brepper tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10_fine.stl -o out.step
  --compare tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10.step` now prints a `Stage 4.1
  WARNING` from the round-trip self-check (re-read the written STEP, compare
  its volume to the in-memory solid). The self-check is gated behind
  `--compare`, which already re-reads the output, so a normal run pays no
  extra STEP read. Per-face inspection tool used for the diagnosis was a
  throwaway bin reading the STEP back via `read_step_file` +
  `top_exp::Explorer` + `BRepAdaptor_Surface` +
  `BRepTools::uv_bounds_face_real4`. Environment: OCCT 7.9.3.
  `occt_full_torus` (complete donut) round-trips fine and
  `pipe_elbow_10_medium` passes, so this is specific to a *partial*
  periodic-torus assembly at fine tessellation.
  `pipe_elbow_10_fine` is NOT a tracked test (no suite regression), but it
  blocks any future fine-torus `--compare` test.
  - **Known OCCT bug; the real fix is an OCCT upgrade (2026-07-21 web
    search + probe).** brepper bundles OCCT **7.9.3** (opencascade-rs
    occt-sys, pinned to the `V7_9_3` tag); the current OCCT release is
    **8.0.0** (May 2026) / 8.0.0_p1 (June 2026). The 8.0.0 release notes
    list a fix that matches this reproduction closely — *"partial torus
    creation handles inverted V range"* — plus *"periodic BSpline curve
    bounding works with the corrected tolerance"* and *"unstable PCurve
    processing … addressed during shape healing"*. Our case is exactly a
    partial torus whose fine-mesh V-range starts slightly negative (an
    inverted/offset V range). **Next step: bump the bundled OCCT to
    8.0.0_p1** (in the sibling opencascade-rs repo — crosses a major
    version, so expect FFI-binding churn) and re-check the round-trip; this
    likely fixes it with no brepper geometry change. (Not confirmed against
    this exact repro — verify.)
  - **The `write.surfacecurve.mode` workaround was TRIED (2026-07-21) and
    does NOT work — do not retry.** Set `write.surfacecurve.mode=0` (verified
    the written STEP then had zero PCURVE/SEAM_CURVE/SURFACE_CURVE entities,
    only 3D curves) and additionally `read.surfacecurve.mode=3`: the
    re-imported volume was still 3087 mm³ (14% low). So the degeneracy is
    NOT in the writer's pcurve→B-spline conversion — OCCT *rebuilds* the
    pcurve from the exact 3D curve on read and that reconstruction ALSO
    degenerates for this partial-torus periodic face. The bug is in OCCT's
    pcurve computation for the partial torus, hit on both the write and read
    paths, which is why only the OCCT upgrade (or generating the geometry so
    the periodic domain lands exactly on `[0, 2π]`) can fix it.
    `interface::Static::set_i_val(name, val)` is the available binding if a
    future attempt needs it.

- Add full-pipeline tests for nosecone_truncated and hill_with_hole — both
```

**After:**

- **FIXED (2026-07-22, OCCT 8.0.0_p1 upgrade): partial-torus STEP round-trip

  the fine pipe-elbow re-read 14% low (3087 vs 3590 mm³) because OCCT's
  pcurve computation for the partial (bend-segment) torus degenerated on

  diff is now 7.6e-9, and `pipe_elbow_10_fine --compare` is a tracked
  regression guard (commit 71a2735). The `write.surfacecurve.mode=0`
  workaround did NOT help and was abandoned. Full diagnosis is in the
  commit history / memory if the failure ever recurs.

1	//! Stage 2.2: region extraction — connected components of the face graph
2	//! over INTERIOR edges (union-find). Regions are maximal, mesh-connected by
3	//! construction, and every face belongs to exactly one region (a single
4	//! facet is a valid region — the terminal per-facet-plane fallback of the
5	//! classification ladder depends on that). Region numbering is
6	//! deterministic: regions are ordered by their smallest member face index.
7	
8	use super::edges::EdgeClassification;
9	use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
10	
11	/// One region of the partition: a maximal set of faces connected across
12	/// INTERIOR edges.
13	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
14	pub struct Region {
15	    /// Member mesh face indices, ascending.
16	    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
17	    /// Member mesh vertex indices (union of face vertices), sorted, deduped.
18	    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
19	}
20	
21	fn find(parent: &mut [usize], mut x: usize) -> usize {
22	    while parent[x] != x {
23	        parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]; // path halving
24	        x = parent[x];
25	    }
26	    x
27	}
28	
29	/// Extract regions from the edge classification. Returns
30	/// \`(face_region, regions)\` where \`face_region[fi]\` is the region id of
31	/// face \`fi\`.
32	pub fn extract_regions(
33	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
34	    edge_classes: &EdgeClassification,
35	) -> (Vec<usize>, Vec<Region>) {
36	    let n = mesh.faces.len();
37	    let mut parent: Vec<usize> = (0..n).collect();
38	
39	    for fi in 0..n {
40	        let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
41	        for e in 0..vc {
42	            let ni = mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[e];
43	            if ni >= 0 && !edge_classes.is_boundary(fi, e) {
44	                let ra = find(&mut parent, fi);
45	                let rb = find(&mut parent, ni as usize);
46	                if ra != rb {
47	                    // Union by smaller root index keeps numbering stable.
48	                    parent[ra.max(rb)] = ra.min(rb);
49	                }
50	            }
51	        }
52	    }
53	
54	    let mut face_region = vec![usize::MAX; n];
55	    let mut region_of_root = vec![usize::MAX; n];
56	    let mut regions: Vec<Region> = Vec::new();
57	    for (fi, slot) in face_region.iter_mut().enumerate() {
58	        let root = find(&mut parent, fi);
59	        let id = if region_of_root[root] == usize::MAX {
60	            regions.push(Region::default());
61	            region_of_root[root] = regions.len() - 1;
62	            regions.len() - 1
63	        } else {
64	            region_of_root[root]
65	        };
66	        *slot = id;
67	        regions[id].faces.push(fi);
68	    }
69	
70	    for region in &mut regions {
71	        let mut vertices: Vec<usize> = region
72	            .faces
73	            .iter()
74	            .flat_map(|&fi| {
75	                let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
76	                mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc].iter().copied()
77	            })
78	            .collect();
79	        vertices.sort_unstable();
80	        vertices.dedup();
81	        region.vertices = vertices;
82	    }
83	
84	    (face_region, regions)
85	}
86

694	fn choose_corners(
695	    lp: &[(usize, usize)],
696	    positions: &[[f64; 3]],
697	) -> Vec<usize> {
698	    let n = lp.len();
699	    // Cumulative chord length at each loop position.
700	    let mut cum = vec![0.0_f64; n + 1];
701	    for i in 0..n {
702	        let a = positions[lp[i].0];
703	        let b = positions[lp[(i + 1) % n].0];
704	        let d = ((a[0] - b[0]).powi(2) + (a[1] - b[1]).powi(2) + (a[2] - b[2]).powi(2)).sqrt();
705	        cum[i + 1] = cum[i] + d;
706	    }
707	    let total = cum[n];
708	
709	    // Outside-surface transitions: vertex i sits between boundary edge i-1
710	    // and edge i; it is a corner candidate when their outside ids differ.
711	    let mut candidates: Vec<usize> = (0..n)
712	        .filter(|&i| lp[(i + n - 1) % n].1 != lp[i].1)
713	        .collect();
714	
715	    if candidates.len() == 4 {
716	        return candidates;
717	    }
718	    if candidates.is_empty() {
719	        candidates.push(0);
720	    }
721	    // Anchor at the first candidate, then pick the loop positions closest to
722	    // the quarter arc-length marks (from remaining candidates when there are
723	    // too many, from any loop vertex when there are too few).
724	    let anchor = candidates[0];
725	    let mut chosen = vec![anchor];
726	    let pool: Vec<usize> = if candidates.len() > 4 {
727	        candidates.clone()
728	    } else {
729	        (0..n).collect()
730	    };
731	    for k in 1..4 {
732	        let target = (cum[anchor] + total * k as f64 / 4.0) % total;
733	        let best = pool
734	            .iter()
735	            .copied()
736	            .filter(|i| !chosen.contains(i))
737	            .min_by(|&a, &b| {

745	    }

747	    chosen.dedup();
748	    chosen

3	//! After stages 2.1–2.6, faces that fit no analytic surface remain as

6	//!
7	//! 1. Parameterize the region onto the unit square with Floater mean-value
8	//!    coordinates (boundary chord-length-pinned to the square perimeter,
9	//!    interior solved by Gauss–Seidel).
10	//! 2. Resample a regular UV grid of 3D points from the parameterized mesh
11	//!    (piecewise-linear, i.e. chordal, between vertices; hole regions filled
12	//!    with a Laplacian membrane so the approximator sees smooth data).
13	//! 3. Fit with \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface\` and validate every region
14	//!    vertex and face centroid against \`--surface-tolerance\`.
15	//!
16	//! The grid is oriented so the fitted surface's natural normal (d1u × d1v)
17	//! matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always

382	/// Connected components of a face set over shared mesh edges.
383	fn connected_components(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
384	    let member: std::collections::HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
385	    let mut seen: std::collections::HashSet<usize> = std::collections::HashSet::new();
386	    let mut components: Vec<Vec<usize>> = Vec::new();
387	    for &start in faces {
388	        if seen.contains(&start) {
389	            continue;
390	        }
391	        let mut comp = Vec::new();
392	        let mut stack = vec![start];
393	        seen.insert(start);
394	        while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
395	            comp.push(fi);
396	            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
397	            for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
398	                if ni >= 0 {
399	                    let ni = ni as usize;
400	                    if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
401	                        stack.push(ni);
402	                    }
403	                }
404	            }
405	        }
406	        comp.sort_unstable();
407	        components.push(comp);
408	    }
409	    components
410	}

        SelectedSurface::BSpline(_) => unreachable!("no bspline hypotheses in stage 2.3/2.4 yet"),
    }
}
```

**After:**
```
fn surface_faces(cls: &Classification, si: usize) -> &[usize] {
    match cls.selected[si] {
        SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &cls.planar[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &cls.conical[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &cls.spherical[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &cls.toroidal[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &cls.bspline[i].faces,
    }
}

fn surface_faces_mut(cls: &mut Classification, si: usize) -> &mut Vec<usize> {
    match cls.selected[si] {
        SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => &mut cls.planar[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => &mut cls.conical[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => &mut cls.spherical[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::Toroidal(i) => &mut cls.toroidal[i].faces,
        SelectedSurface::BSpline(i) => &mut cls.bspline[i].faces,
    }
}

/// A B-spline surface is inert in repair: its fit vouches only for its own
/// data, so relabel/merge/re-split never move faces into or out of it (a
/// moved face would invalidate the fit and its cached projector). All three
/// operations skip B-spline surfaces via this predicate.
fn is_bspline(cls: &Classification, si: usize) -> bool {
    matches!(cls.selected[si], SelectedSurface::BSpline(_))
}
```

pub(super) fn classify_regions(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    regions: &[Region],
    jet: &[JetField],
    config: &Config,
) -> Classification {
    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
        .faces
        .iter()
        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    let mut cls = Classification::new();
    let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
    let mut competed_regions = 0usize;
    let mut competed_surfaces = 0usize;
    let mut facet_faces = 0usize;
    for region in regions {
        if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
            continue;
        }
        // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
        competed_regions += 1;
        let (hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);

        // A whole undecomposed sub-surface left as per-facet planes (e.g. a
        // coarse fillet competition cannot fit) reconstructs to invalid wires
        // where it abuts a committed analytic surface — worse than a clean
        // polyhedron. When the largest leftover blob is more than a junction
        // sliver, discard competition and per-facet the whole region; a clean
        // polyhedron is the safe fallback until freeform fitting (2.3(e))
        // gives those blobs a principled home. (Healthy competition leaves
        // only tiny scattered slivers at junctions.)
        if largest_component(mesh, &leftovers) > LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX {
            for &fi in &region.faces {
                cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
            }
            facet_faces += region.faces.len();
            continue;
        }

        competed_surfaces += hyps.len();
        for hyp in hyps {
            cls.push_compete(hyp);
        }
        // Whatever competition left unclaimed → per-facet planes (total
        // coverage can never fail; a single triangle is exactly a plane).
        facet_faces += leftovers.len();
        for fi in leftovers {
            cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
        }
    }

    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; competition: {} region(s) → {} surfaces + {} per-facet",
            ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3],
            competed_regions, competed_surfaces, facet_faces,
        );
    }
    cls
}
```

**After:**
```
pub(super) fn classify_regions(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    regions: &[Region],
    jet: &[JetField],
    face_region: &[usize],
    config: &Config,
) -> Classification {
    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
        .faces
        .iter()
        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    let mut cls = Classification::new();
    let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
    let mut competed_regions = 0usize;
    let mut competed_surfaces = 0usize;
    let mut freeform_surfaces = 0usize;
    let mut facet_faces = 0usize;
    for region in regions {
        if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
            continue;
        }
        // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
        competed_regions += 1;
        let (hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);

        // Stage 2.3(e): freeform-fit the leftover components competition
        // could not explain analytically, dissolving any small analytic
        // island enclosed by a validating B-spline patch.
        let ff = bspline::fit_leftover_components(mesh, &leftovers, &hyps, face_region, config);

        // A committed analytic surface islanded among a large jagged
        // per-facet blob reconstructs to invalid wires where they abut —
        // worse than a clean polyhedron. When an analytic hypothesis
        // survives AND freeform could not clear a big leftover blob, discard
        // competition and per-facet the whole region. (Healthy competition
        // leaves only tiny scattered slivers, and freeform absorbs genuine
        // freeform patches, so this fires only on undecomposable coarse
        // fillets.)
        let surviving_hyps = hyps.len() - ff.dissolved.len();
        if surviving_hyps > 0 && largest_component(mesh, &ff.residual) > LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX {
            for &fi in &region.faces {
                cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
            }
            facet_faces += region.faces.len();
            continue;
        }

        // Commit surviving analytic hypotheses (skipping dissolved islands),
        // the freeform surfaces, and the residual leftovers as per-facet
        // planes (total coverage can never fail; a triangle is exactly a
        // plane).
        for (i, hyp) in hyps.into_iter().enumerate() {
            if !ff.dissolved.contains(&i) {
                cls.push_compete(hyp);
                competed_surfaces += 1;
            }
        }
        for plane in ff.planes {
            cls.push_planar(plane);
        }
        for hyp in ff.bsplines {
            cls.push_bspline(hyp);
            freeform_surfaces += 1;
        }
        facet_faces += ff.residual.len();
        for fi in ff.residual {
            cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
        }
    }

    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; competition: {} region(s) → {} analytic + {} freeform + {} per-facet",
            ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3],
            competed_regions, competed_surfaces, freeform_surfaces, facet_faces,
        );
    }
    cls
}
```
</details>

/// selections and fit each with a B-spline surface (or, for regions that are
/// planar to fitting exactness, a multi-face plane). Successful fits replace
/// the component's single-face planar selections in `selected_surfaces`.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
pub(super) fn deduce_bspline_surfaces(
```

**After:**
```
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Stage 2.3(e): freeform leftovers (the rewrite's freeform rung)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// One leftover component's freeform fit: a multi-face plane (when the
/// component is planar to fitting exactness) or one-or-two B-spline surfaces
/// (two only for a seam-split closed band).
enum ComponentFit {
    Plane(PlanarHypothesis),
    Bsplines(Vec<BSplineHypothesis>),
}

/// Result of freeform-fitting the unclaimed leftover components of one
/// competed region (stage 2.3(e)).
pub(super) struct FreeformResult {
    /// Freeform B-spline surfaces (one per fitted component; two for a
    /// seam-split closed band).
    pub bsplines: Vec<BSplineHypothesis>,
    /// Multi-face planar hypotheses for components that turned out planar to
    /// fitting exactness (a plane beats a B-spline that overfits noise).
    pub planes: Vec<PlanarHypothesis>,
    /// Indices into the competition `hyps` slice of small analytic islands
    /// dissolved into a freeform patch (the caller drops them).
    pub dissolved: HashSet<usize>,
    /// Leftover faces that remain per-facet planes: components below the
    /// minimum fit size, and fits that failed at every grid density.
    pub residual: Vec<usize>,
}

/// Stage 2.3(e): fit the unclaimed leftover components of a competed region
/// with B-spline surfaces (the reborn NURBS rung — the old region-*gathering*
/// front end is gone; the smooth-region partition and competition leftovers
/// supply the input directly).
///
/// Connected components of `leftovers` at or above `MIN_BSPLINE_FACES` are
/// fitted; a component planar to fitting exactness becomes a multi-face plane
/// instead. Before fitting, small committed analytic islands (`hyps`) fully
/// enclosed by a component are provisionally dissolved into it and kept
/// freeform only if the fit then validates their vertices too, else restored
/// (the fit-validated reincarnation of the old gathering's confetti-absorption
/// guard). Components below the minimum and fits that fail at every grid
/// density degrade to per-facet planes (`residual`) — total coverage can
/// never fail.
///
/// `face_region` (region id per mesh face) is the outside-surface key for
/// boundary-corner detection: distinct neighbouring regions (the freeform
/// patch's walls) carry distinct ids, so corners land where the adjacent
/// surface changes.
pub(super) fn fit_leftover_components(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    leftovers: &[usize],
    hyps: &[CompeteHyp],
    face_region: &[usize],
    config: &Config,
) -> FreeformResult {
    let mut result = FreeformResult {
        bsplines: Vec::new(),
        planes: Vec::new(),
        dissolved: HashSet::new(),
        residual: Vec::new(),
    };

    // Small analytic hypotheses eligible for island dissolution.
    let small_hyps: Vec<(usize, &[usize])> = hyps
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .filter(|(_, h)| h.faces().len() <= SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES)
        .map(|(i, h)| (i, h.faces()))
        .collect();

    for comp in leftover_components(mesh, leftovers) {
        if comp.len() < MIN_BSPLINE_FACES {
            result.residual.extend(comp);
            continue;
        }
        let comp_set: HashSet<usize> = comp.iter().copied().collect();

        // Islands enclosed by this component (and not already dissolved).
        let islands: Vec<usize> = small_hyps
            .iter()
            .filter(|(i, faces)| {
                !result.dissolved.contains(i) && island_enclosed(mesh, faces, &comp_set)
            })
            .map(|(i, _)| *i)
            .collect();

        // Attempt 1: dissolve the enclosed islands into the component. If the
        // B-spline validates the whole patch including the island vertices,
        // the islands stay freeform (and the caller drops them).
        if !islands.is_empty() {
            let mut augmented = comp.clone();
            for &i in &islands {
                let faces = small_hyps.iter().find(|(j, _)| *j == i).unwrap().1;
                augmented.extend_from_slice(faces);
            }
            augmented.sort_unstable();
            augmented.dedup();
            if let Some(fit) = fit_or_planarize(mesh, &augmented, face_region, config) {
                commit_component_fit(fit, &mut result);
                result.dissolved.extend(islands);
                continue;
            }
        }

        // Attempt 2 (or the only attempt): fit the component itself; enclosed
        // islands stay analytic and appear as holes in the flattening.
        match fit_or_planarize(mesh, &comp, face_region, config) {
            Some(fit) => commit_component_fit(fit, &mut result),
            None => result.residual.extend(comp),
        }
    }
    result
}

fn commit_component_fit(fit: ComponentFit, result: &mut FreeformResult) {
    match fit {
        ComponentFit::Plane(p) => result.planes.push(p),
        ComponentFit::Bsplines(bs) => result.bsplines.extend(bs),
    }
}

/// Explain one leftover component: a plane when it is planar to fitting
/// exactness (10× vertex tolerance — a shallow dome is NOT flattened),
/// otherwise a B-spline fit. Returns `None` when the B-spline fit fails at
/// every grid density (the component degrades to per-facet planes).
fn fit_or_planarize(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    comp: &[usize],
    face_region: &[usize],
    config: &Config,
) -> Option<ComponentFit> {
    let vert_set: HashSet<usize> = comp
        .iter()
        .flat_map(|&fi| {
            let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
            f.vertex_indices[..f.vertex_count as usize].to_vec()
        })
        .collect();

    // Planar to fitting exactness → a plane, not a B-spline that overfits
    // noise. The area-weighted normal of a closed/symmetric region cancels to
    // ~zero and normalizes to NaN; treat any non-finite plane as non-planar.
    let (pn, pd) = planar::fit_plane(comp, &vert_set, &mesh.faces, &mesh.vertices);
    let plane_tol = 10.0 * config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
    let plane_err = if pn.iter().all(|c| c.is_finite()) && pd.is_finite() {
        vert_set
            .iter()
            .map(|&vi| {
                let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
                (pn[0] * v.x + pn[1] * v.y + pn[2] * v.z - pd).abs()
            })
            .fold(0.0_f64, f64::max)
    } else {
        f64::INFINITY
    };
    if plane_err <= plane_tol {
        let mut verts: Vec<usize> = vert_set.into_iter().collect();
        verts.sort_unstable();
        return Some(ComponentFit::Plane(PlanarHypothesis {
            normal: pn,
            distance: pd,
            faces: comp.to_vec(),
            vertices: verts,
            error_max: plane_err,
            error_min: -plane_err,
            error_abs_sum: 0.0,
        }));
    }

    let n = mesh.faces.len();
    let comp_set: HashSet<usize> = comp.iter().copied().collect();
    let in_region: Vec<bool> = (0..n).map(|fi| comp_set.contains(&fi)).collect();
    match fit_component(mesh, comp.to_vec(), face_region, &in_region, config) {
        Ok(hyps) => Some(ComponentFit::Bsplines(hyps)),
        Err(reason) => {
            if config.verbosity >= 1 {
                eprintln!(
                    "  Stage 2.3(e): {}-face freeform component left as per-facet planes: {reason}",
                    comp.len(),
                );
            }
            None
        }
    }
}

/// Connected components (over shared mesh edges) of a leftover face set, each
/// ascending, outer vec ordered by smallest member face.
fn leftover_components(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
    let member: HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
    let mut out: Vec<Vec<usize>> = Vec::new();
    let mut starts = faces.to_vec();
    starts.sort_unstable();
    for &start in &starts {
        if seen.contains(&start) {
            continue;
        }
        let mut comp = Vec::new();
        let mut stack = vec![start];
        seen.insert(start);
        while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
            comp.push(fi);
            let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
            for k in 0..f.vertex_count as usize {
                let ni = f.neighbors[k];
                if ni >= 0 {
                    let ni = ni as usize;
                    if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
                        stack.push(ni);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        comp.sort_unstable();
        out.push(comp);
    }
    out
}

/// Whether every face of `island` has all its neighbours inside the island or
/// inside `comp_set` — i.e. the island is fully surrounded by the leftover
/// component, so dissolving it cannot strand a third surface. An island
/// touching an open edge or a different surface is not enclosed.
fn island_enclosed(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, island: &[usize], comp_set: &HashSet<usize>) -> bool {
    let island_set: HashSet<usize> = island.iter().copied().collect();
    for &fi in island {
        let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
        for k in 0..f.vertex_count as usize {
            let ni = f.neighbors[k];
            if ni < 0 {
                return false;
            }
            let ni = ni as usize;
            if !island_set.contains(&ni) && !comp_set.contains(&ni) {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }
    true
}

/// selections and fit each with a B-spline surface (or, for regions that are
/// planar to fitting exactness, a multi-face plane). Successful fits replace
/// the component's single-face planar selections in `selected_surfaces`.

This is more restrictive. Now I'm reconsidering whether to even fit single-only components in cases where the absorbed portion is smaller than the single leftover and we have a pure freeform region. When competition leaves a large single leftover with small absorbed pieces, we fit the composite. But for regions where competition committed a larger portion...
> 
> Writing the component filtering logic... Continuing the fit logic...
> 
> When a fit succeeds, I commit it and then check if we're dissolving the absorbed component—if so, I mark those small hypotheses as dissolved. If fitting fails, the single faces go into the residual pile. I'm also realizing I need to track which faces belong to which small hypothesis so I can properly record dissolutions, using a map where usize::MAX means no hypothesis.
> 
> For the edge case where leftover faces form a small freeform patch (exactly 4 faces) surrounded by analytic geometry, I should fit just those 4 faces as a bspline if the surrounding absorbed component is larger. This replaces my earlier island_enclosed logic entirely, so I need to clean that up and rewrite fit_leftover_components. But now I'm hitting a concern about how domain-based component merging interacts with the fit_component function itself.
> 
> When fitting a component that mixes leftover and small analytic faces, the analytic faces get interspersed throughout. The boundary loops built from the component's edges should flatten correctly since the component is a connected set within the smooth region—its boundary is essentially the region's outer perimeter plus any holes. For the arch example, the whole region becomes one component of 48 faces, which is the same as fitting the entire arch. The fit_component function uses face_to_selected (mapped to face_region) to identify outside neighbors, so for the arch component, boundary neighbors are walls with distinct region IDs, which correctly produces corners.
> 
> But there's a subtlety: domain includes small analytic faces even for components where absorbed > single, meaning we won't absorb them. In those cases, fit_faces only contains the single (leftover) faces, which might themselves be disconnected.
> 
> If single is disconnected, fit_component fails and we residual everything—an edge case but acceptable. Now checking the wave case: competition's committed planes are all tiny (3, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1 faces), so they're all in domain, and single dominates at ~72 faces. Absorbing everything yields one bspline and dissolves all planes. But I need to verify whether competition commits only small planes for the wave or if there's something larger than 16 faces that's outside the domain and would cause fragmentation. Now I'm refactoring `fit_leftover_components` to use the domain approach and removing the unused `island_enclosed` helper while keeping `ComponentFit`. I also need to reconsider the safety net in `classify.rs` — with the domain approach, `dissolved` tracks indices of small dissolved hypersurfaces, so `surviving_hyps` equals the total count minus dissolved count. For the arch case that's 0, but for a coarse fillet where competition commits real analytic surfaces (some too large to dissolve) and the fillet itself is leftover, the freeform might fit the fillet with 0 dissolved, yet bsplines still has 1 surface to account for.

pub(super) fn fit_leftover_components(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    leftovers: &[usize],
    hyps: &[CompeteHyp],
    face_region: &[usize],
    config: &Config,
) -> FreeformResult {
    let mut result = FreeformResult {
        bsplines: Vec::new(),
        planes: Vec::new(),
        dissolved: HashSet::new(),
        residual: Vec::new(),
    };

    // Small analytic hypotheses eligible for island dissolution.
    let small_hyps: Vec<(usize, &[usize])> = hyps
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .filter(|(_, h)| h.faces().len() <= SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES)
        .map(|(i, h)| (i, h.faces()))
        .collect();

    for comp in leftover_components(mesh, leftovers) {
        if comp.len() < MIN_BSPLINE_FACES {
            result.residual.extend(comp);
            continue;
        }
        let comp_set: HashSet<usize> = comp.iter().copied().collect();

        // Islands enclosed by this component (and not already dissolved).
        let islands: Vec<usize> = small_hyps
            .iter()
            .filter(|(i, faces)| {
                !result.dissolved.contains(i) && island_enclosed(mesh, faces, &comp_set)
            })
            .map(|(i, _)| *i)
            .collect();

        // Attempt 1: dissolve the enclosed islands into the component. If the
        // B-spline validates the whole patch including the island vertices,
        // the islands stay freeform (and the caller drops them).
        if !islands.is_empty() {
            let mut augmented = comp.clone();
            for &i in &islands {
                let faces = small_hyps.iter().find(|(j, _)| *j == i).unwrap().1;
                augmented.extend_from_slice(faces);
            }
            augmented.sort_unstable();
            augmented.dedup();
            if let Some(fit) = fit_or_planarize(mesh, &augmented, face_region, config) {
                commit_component_fit(fit, &mut result);
                result.dissolved.extend(islands);
                continue;
            }
        }

        // Attempt 2 (or the only attempt): fit the component itself; enclosed
        // islands stay analytic and appear as holes in the flattening.
        match fit_or_planarize(mesh, &comp, face_region, config) {
            Some(fit) => commit_component_fit(fit, &mut result),
            None => result.residual.extend(comp),
        }
    }
    result
}
```

**After:**
```
pub(super) fn fit_leftover_components(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    leftovers: &[usize],
    hyps: &[CompeteHyp],
    face_region: &[usize],
    config: &Config,
) -> FreeformResult {
    let mut result = FreeformResult {
        bsplines: Vec::new(),
        planes: Vec::new(),
        dissolved: HashSet::new(),
        residual: Vec::new(),
    };

    // A freeform region baits the analytic detectors locally: an inflection
    // grows a run of accidentally-coplanar facets as a tiny plane, and a
    // singly-curved patch grows a short circular-cylinder band — each
    // *fragmenting* the region into separate leftover pieces. So the freeform
    // domain is the leftover faces PLUS the small analytic islands
    // (≤ SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES) that could be such accidents; growing
    // components over that domain reunites the pieces the islands split. A
    // committed island whose faces this rung absorbs into a validating patch
    // is dissolved (the caller drops it). `face_hyp[fi]` = the small hyp
    // owning face `fi` (or usize::MAX).
    let mut face_hyp = vec![usize::MAX; mesh.faces.len()];
    let mut domain: Vec<usize> = leftovers.to_vec();
    for (hi, h) in hyps.iter().enumerate() {
        if h.faces().len() <= SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES {
            for &fi in h.faces() {
                face_hyp[fi] = hi;
                domain.push(fi);
            }
        }
    }
    domain.sort_unstable();
    domain.dedup();
    let leftover_set: HashSet<usize> = leftovers.iter().copied().collect();

    for comp in leftover_components(mesh, &domain) {
        // Unexplained (leftover) freeform faces in this component.
        let single: Vec<usize> = comp
            .iter()
            .copied()
            .filter(|fi| leftover_set.contains(fi))
            .collect();
        // A component with too little unexplained freeform is not a freeform
        // patch — a real small analytic feature competition fit, or a junction
        // sliver. Leave the islands committed; the leftover faces go per-facet.
        if single.len() < MIN_BSPLINE_FACES {
            result.residual.extend(single);
            continue;
        }

        // Absorb the small analytic islands into the freeform patch only when
        // the unexplained area dominates (evidence the islands are accidents);
        // otherwise trust the analytic fits and fit just the leftover freeform.
        let absorbed = comp.len() - single.len();
        let (fit_faces, dissolve) = if absorbed <= single.len() {
            (comp.clone(), true)
        } else {
            (single.clone(), false)
        };

        match fit_or_planarize(mesh, &fit_faces, face_region, config) {
            Some(fit) => {
                commit_component_fit(fit, &mut result);
                if dissolve {
                    for &fi in &comp {
                        if face_hyp[fi] != usize::MAX {
                            result.dissolved.insert(face_hyp[fi]);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            None => result.residual.extend(single),
        }
    }
    result
}
```
</details>

```
/// Whether every face of `island` has all its neighbours inside the island or
/// inside `comp_set` — i.e. the island is fully surrounded by the leftover
/// component, so dissolving it cannot strand a third surface. An island
/// touching an open edge or a different surface is not enclosed.
fn island_enclosed(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, island: &[usize], comp_set: &HashSet<usize>) -> bool {
    let island_set: HashSet<usize> = island.iter().copied().collect();
    for &fi in island {
        let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
        for k in 0..f.vertex_count as usize {
            let ni = f.neighbors[k];
            if ni < 0 {
                return false;
            }
            let ni = ni as usize;
            if !island_set.contains(&ni) && !comp_set.contains(&ni) {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }
    true
}

6. **Freeform rewiring** (`bspline.rs`: leftover components +
   island-dissolution guard). Acceptance: tests/nurbs suite green. **[DONE.]**
   As built: `fit_leftover_components` (a NEW front end; the old region
   *gathering* still lives beside it until the step-7 deletion) fits the
   unclaimed leftover components of each competed region. The freeform
   *domain* is the leftover faces PLUS the small (≤ 16-face) analytic islands
   competition committed — a singly-curved patch grows a short accidental
   cylinder band (the arch) and an inflection grows accidental coplanar
   strips (the wave), each *fragmenting* the region into separate leftover
   pieces; growing connected components over that domain reunites them.
   Per component: if the unexplained (leftover) area dominates
   (`absorbed ≤ single`), the islands are absorbed into the fit and, if it
   validates, dissolved (dropped by the caller); otherwise the analytic fits
   are trusted and only the leftover freeform is fitted. Components whose
   leftover part is below `MIN_BSPLINE_FACES` (4), and fits that fail at
   every grid density, degrade to per-facet planes. `face_region` (region id
   per mesh face) is the outside-surface key for boundary-corner detection.
   The old `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX` whole-region discard is now the *post-freeform*
   safety net: it fires only when a committed analytic surface survives AND
   freeform could not clear a big leftover blob — so a partially-decomposed
   model (fillet tori + an undecomposed blob) keeps its analytic surfaces
   once freeform gives the blob a home, instead of discarding them
   (filleted_hole_block, ccad_cone_cylinder params, filleted_cylinder tori
   all recovered). B-spline surfaces are inert in stage-2.4 repair (never a

Stage 2 rewrite: step 6 (freeform rewiring — leftover B-spline fitting)

Wire the freeform (B-spline) rung into the new partition-then-classify
pipeline (stage 2.3(e)). Competition leftovers — the faces no analytic
surface explained — now go to `bspline::fit_leftover_components` instead of
straight to per-facet planes; the old region-*gathering* front end
(`deduce_bspline_surfaces`) stays beside it, unused by the new pipeline,
until the step-7 deletion pass.

- `fit_leftover_components` (bspline.rs): the freeform *domain* is the
  leftover faces PLUS the small (≤ SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES) analytic
  islands competition committed. A singly-curved patch grows a short
  accidental circular-cylinder band (elliptical_arch) and an inflection
  grows accidental coplanar strips (bspline_wave), each fragmenting the
  freeform region into separate leftover pieces; growing connected
  components over the domain reunites them. Per component, when the
  unexplained (leftover) area dominates (`absorbed ≤ single`), the islands
  are absorbed into the fit and, if it validates, dissolved (the caller
  drops those competition hypotheses); otherwise the analytic fits are
  trusted and only the leftover freeform is fitted. Components whose
  leftover part is below MIN_BSPLINE_FACES, and fits that fail at every grid
  density, degrade to per-facet planes. Reuses the existing `fit_component`
  machinery unchanged, with the region-id map as the boundary-corner
  outside-surface key.
- classify.rs: the `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX` whole-region discard becomes a
  POST-freeform safety net — it fires only when a committed analytic
  surface survives AND freeform could not clear a big leftover blob. So a
  partially decomposed model (fillet tori + an undecomposed blob) keeps its
  analytic surfaces once freeform gives the blob a home, instead of
  discarding them.
- repair.rs (stage 2.4): B-spline surfaces are inert — never a relabel,
  merge, or re-split target (a fit vouches only for its own data). The
  surface accessors now return real B-spline faces (for coverage /
  connectivity invariants) and the `unreachable!` panics are gone.
- mod.rs: thread `face_region` into `classify_regions` and route
  `cls.bspline` into `Stage2Output.bspline_hypotheses`.

Acceptance (tests/nurbs stage-2 selection) green: elliptical_arch → 1
B-spline + 3 walls, bspline_wave → 1 B-spline + 5 walls,
elliptical_cylinder → 1 periodic B-spline band + 2 caps, chamfered_cube
untouched. Net vs the pre-step-6 baseline, zero regressions:
stage2_integration 132→139, stage3 99→101, stage4 28→31 (filleted_hole_block,
ccad_cone_cylinder params, filleted_cylinder tori recovered because their
leftover blob now gets a freeform home rather than forcing an analytic
discard); lib 90, stage1 83, seg 14, competition 3 all green. Remaining
stage3/4 failures are the documented pre-existing set (part_rounded_cube +
rounded_cube coarse; ccad cone_cylinder/filleted_cylinder/filleted_pipe
reference mismatches; stepped_block_toroidal coarse/medium).

Also prunes the now-stale pipe_elbow_10_fine STEP-export TODO item (fixed by
the OCCT 8.0.0_p1 upgrade) to a one-line resolved note, and refreshes the
CURRENT WORK item to point at steps 6/7.
